# Testing

Runner: `node --test` (bare - a trailing `test/` path breaks discovery on
Node 22). Suite: 3451 tests across 350 files.

**THE ARENA2-GATED PINS ARE HALF-BLIND, AND THAT IS A KNOWN COST.** A
pin behind `{ skip: skipReal }` never runs on CI, so a law change that
contradicts it goes unnoticed until someone runs the suite WITH game
data. Two did (2026-08-23, found while merging the input arc):
audit18_hosts_dungeon's swing-fatigue pin still asserted the drain
AUDIT 24 wave 42 had deliberately moved to the hosts, and
cityguards.test.js's stub renderer lacked the `destroyBillboardBatch`
wave 6's batch-free calls - it THREW rather than failed. Both are
fixed, and both by making the pin say MORE, not less. When you change
a law, grep the ARENA2-gated files for it too.

**AND THEY ARE ENGLISH-RETAIL-LOCKED, WHICH NOTHING SAID.** 2026-08-25:
the gated pins ran in a working environment for the first time, against
a real ARENA2 pulled from the DaggerfallSetup installer. 35 of them
failed, and NONE of the 35 is a port defect. 8 fail on FILENAME CASE
alone - retail data was written on a filesystem that did not care, and
that install carries `INVE00I0.img` beside `INVE04I0.IMG` and
`ANIM0001.vid` beside `ANIM0002.VID`, 76 lowercase names among 1,598.
The shipping data path already knew (`dataSource.normalizeName`
uppercases on both ingest and lookup, so the browser is fine); the test
harness and the vite dev middleware read the folder case-sensitively,
so `npm run dev` 404'd against files that were sitting right there.
The dev server is case-insensitive now; the pins still read the folder
literally and a mixed-case corpus still reddens them.

The other 27 are LOCALISATION. That install is the German edition, and
these pins encode the English retail corpus by value: FACTION.TXT has
374 records against the pinned 366 and names faction 41 Kriegergilde,
the map directory has no location called "Privateer's Hold" so five
dungeon pins dereference null, SPELLS.STD's indices move, CLASS*.CFG
carries different career flags (a Mage there CAN wear plate), and the
IMG corpus is 198 files against 263. Every one of those is the data
being legitimately different, not the port being wrong.

Two port-relevant facts fell out of the same run and are recorded
rather than fixed, because both change shipped behaviour: the
CLASS*.CFG NAME FIELD IS ENCIPHERED in that edition (CLASS00 reads
"vfu", CLASS15 reads "dfhdfh" - a repeated three-gram where German's
Barbar is a repeated three-gram, so the record parses correctly and
only the name is coded) and `chargenSession` falls back to
CLASS_CAREERS only when the name is EMPTY, so a non-English install
shows gibberish in the class list; and the port's font maps the
high-bit range to punctuation, so every umlaut renders as `#` or `}`
("St#rke", "W}ste"). Neither can be caught by any pin the suite has,
because the suite has never had non-English data.


| File | Tests | Covers |
|---|---|---|
| action.test.js | 9 | door lifecycle/verbatim constants, move tween + chain gate, activation picking; U6 text actions (the 8600/5400/7700 bases + the verbatim type-12 answers, ShowText firing, ShowTextWithInput skipping the up-front cascade with only a case-insensitive answer match firing ActivateNext, DoorText remapping 7701->7705 + holding the door on first activation + the axisRaw>5 trespass gate); P10 locks (lock value 16 / magic threshold 20, the clamp(5..95) lockpicking chance, all five LookAtInteriorLock text tiers, locked-door refusal firing onLockedDoor while solid, Lock/Unlock/Open/Close delegates through door-riding action nodes incl. only-when-unlocked and StartingLockValue restore) and P10 teleport (resolved next-object warp, null destination refuses, actionless-destination cascade no-op) + the block-instance key namespace (two instances of one RDB position both register and play independently - the 3108-dungeon repeated-block collision fix); AUDIT 23 save-load-11, the ACTION-OBJECT SAVE RECORD run end to end (collectSaveData/restoreSaveData: a Translation-flagged portcullis saved a third of the way up restores a third of the way up with its collider bucket at the restored pose and finishes the remaining 1.8 s, the Move pair is presence-gated so a pre-fix snapshot leaves the live tween alone, and the swing half - state/t/lock/activationCount - still round-trips through the same law) |
| factionrep.test.js | 19 | S25's faction reputation store against DFU's PersistentFactionData. The propagation law needs CONTROLLED trees, so most pins run on synthetic dicts - and the first test asserts a synthetic record carries exactly the field set the real reader mints, so a hand-built fixture cannot describe a shape nothing in the game produces (it caught this file doing exactly that: the zeroing pin hand-built DFU's regionData[].legalRep, which the port never mints, and passed against an implementation reading the same wrong field - a live probe found the pair, and the pin now lets court.js's changeLegalRep MINT the shape). Pinned: the clamp bounds against DFU's LITERALS rather than the port's constants (asserting MIN_POWER let a mutation of that constant move the expectation with it - vacuous, and a mutation run caught it); halving that truncates TOWARD ZERO, the C# int-division quirk every negative half-delta rides on; allies +half and enemies -half, flat, never fanning out again; the Dark Brotherhood treated as its own root although it has a parent; the knightly-order branch; the God test reading the ROOT's type and not the origin's; questors and root parents taking full where siblings take half; and that zeroing RESTORES FACTION.TXT values rather than blanking them. Last, that propagation TERMINATES on the real 366-faction corpus - which is a fact about the DATA, since the walk re-enters itself and only ends because Generic Temple's type is 9 rather than God and 844's ggroup is 17 rather than 9 - asserted BOUNDED so a mutation fails instead of hanging the suite; AUDIT 21 added two: F10 the Generic Temple hop, which was pinned `!== 0` and so left BOTH its amount and its propagate flag free - PersistentFactionData.cs:434-435 passes `amount, true`, and on the real file 450's children include the questor 240, so replacing the call with `(GENERIC_TEMPLE, 1, false)` collapsed five factions and 70 reputation points to one faction and 1 point undetected; F12 the law that DFU has NO visited-set - allies and enemies are paid BEFORE the hierarchy walk, so a faction that is both an ally and a sibling takes the change TWICE (from Molag Bal, Azura reaches +100 and Mephala reaches exactly 0), which is load-bearing on the real Daedra tree at 32 origin/faction pairs and which an 'obvious' de-duplication guard would have broken with every test still passing |
| gamedate.test.js | 9 | S28's calendar against DaggerfallDateTime.cs. The port had a minute counter and no DATE, which is why the guild 28-day rank gate could not tick; the pins are the arithmetic that closes that. SetClassicGameStartTime is pinned against DFU's own docstring - 13:30 on the 4th of Morning Star, 3E405 - as the zero-based field set AND through the 1-based readers, and the class's field defaults are pinned as DIFFERENT from it, because both exist in DFU and collapsing them would lose the distinction between a fresh DaggerfallDateTime and a new game. DayOfYear is swept over all 360 (month, day) pairs to prove it is 1-based, gapless and bijective, so a lost +1 fails rather than shifting quietly. GetSeasonValue keeps DFU's own admitted approximation - clean month boundaries, Evening Star wrapping to Winter - pinned as a twelve-entry table, and the day-name cycle is pinned at the 30-day month's wrap where a naive week count drifts. Last, the reason it landed: daySinceZero over the port's elapsed-minute clock moves exactly one per game day, crosses DAYS_BETWEEN_RANK_CHANGES at 28 and not at 27, and survives a year boundary |
| guilds.test.js | 31 | AUDIT 20 added five: the membership slot is the GUILD GROUP (all eight temples share one, so joining Mara's replaces Arkay's - keyed by name the port held all eight at once), HasJoined taking a GROUP the way DFU asks it, the per-RANK promotion message (five of six guilds announce the benefit the rank unlocked; the port returned one flat record), guildOfFaction resolving temples and orders after G2 shipped them, and a temple faction resolving to HolyOrder through its CHILD because every divine's own ggroup is None. G1's guild foundation against DFU's Guild.cs. Rank is RECOMPUTED from reputation and skills rather than stored, so most pins are table-walk boundaries: the three rank tables deepEqual'd against DFU's literals; a negative reputation expelling before a skill is read; `return --r` giving -1 rather than 0 when row 0 fails; and the `else if` that stops a HIGH skill also counting LOW, pinned by the case where one maxed skill would otherwise satisfy "two skills, one high" alone. The 28-day gate is pinned on days-since-zero so it survives a year boundary, with day 27 moving nothing and day 28 promoting. EXPULSION REMOVES THE MEMBERSHIP, pinned on the KEY rather than the rank - the first cut left removal to the caller, which let the port hold a keyed membership at rank -1, a state DFU never has; a mutation run found it. On the real FACTION.TXT: all four guild faction ids resolve to the right factions, and THE MERCHANTS (510) really do ship ggroup 11 (FightersGuild), which is why DFU hardcodes 510 to None and why every shop would otherwise answer as a Fighters Guild hall + AUDIT 21: two laws the port had no counterpart for. THE THIEVES GUILD AND THE DARK BROTHERHOOD NEVER EXPEL - both override CalculateNewRank with AllowGuildExpulsion, clamping a negative rank to 0, and the port's single shared calculateNewRank DELETED the membership of either at negative reputation, unrecoverably since both are initiation-only. Pinned on both sides: those two clamp to 0, the other four still expel, and the end-to-end updateRank path keeps the record. AND THE JOIN GATES IN DFU'S ORDER - JoinButton_OnMouseClick asks the guild manager for the guild ALREADY IN THAT GROUP SLOT and stops dead if IsMember, so a rank-7 member is offered nothing and a Patriarch of Mara cannot trade her temple for rank 0 in Arkay's; the port ran only the eligibility check, and joinGuild ASSIGNS into the slot. The gate is keyed by GROUP, not guild name - keying it by name passes the same-guild case and still lets the temples trade + guildOfFaction resolving by GUILD GROUP as GetGuild(factionId) does, not by matching a guild's own faction id - 68 more of the corpus's factions now answer, and the pin holds the REAL split rather than pretending everything resolves: 307 factions carry a group, 161 are in one of the six groups CreateGuildObj implements, 98 of those resolve, and the other 63 are sub-factions like 'Apothecaries of Arkay' or 'The Royal Guard' that carry a variant group but are not one of the eight divines or ten orders - Temple.GetDivine THROWS for those and DFU catches it, so null is DFU's answer too. Oblivion's 209 Daedric factions must likewise stay null, because CreateGuildObj's switch ends `default: return null`; AUDIT 21 added seven, six of them the guilds lane's second half: F4 IsSatisfyQuestReqByLevel (the two-guild flag - Mages and the knightly orders - plus questRankFor, the three lines in DaggerfallGuildServicePopupWindow that read it, so a level-12 rank-2 mage draws the level-12 quest pool and a fighter does not); F7 getTitle, which corrected a DOC LIE - the header claimed the player-name fallback was 'DFU's own non-member return' when Temple, KnightlyOrder and DarkBrotherhood each override GetTitle and return a localized "nonMember" string, so the port was shipping a WRONG KNOWN value for half the guilds while claiming to withhold only the unknown one (getTitle answered null there, and the four gendered rank slots were recorded as structure); F8 the initiation quest ids O0A0AL00 and L0A01L00, the ONLY way into the two invitation-only guilds - INVITATION_ONLY had named the refusal and the port had an exit with no entrance; F9 vampMemberships, the second parallel membership book DFU swaps in on vampirism (a plain object still means the mortal book, so no call site broke) and ClearMembershipData clearing BOTH; F10 GetGuildFactionId derived from GUILDS so it cannot drift, including the two deliberate zeros for the variant groups; and F12/F14/F15 the four fixed guilds pinned to LITERALS - which skills, which text records, which promotion maps, which guildGroup - after the lane proved eight separate id-table mutations survived because the pins read their expected value back out of the table under test (M10 was the sharpest: it changed the Mages Guild's default promotion record 5236 and the pin still passed). Two equivalent mutants are recorded in-file so nobody re-hunts them: questRankFor's `>` vs `>=` (Math.max either way) and deleting guildFactionIdOfGroup's variant branch (the derived map misses those groups and falls to the same 0, exactly as DFU's switch falls through to `default:`); U23 met F7 from the other side: widening the DFU sparse clone to Assets/Localization recovered the strings the structure was waiting for, so the same test now reads a title instead of a withheld null - members read RankTitles[rank] in all six guilds, the three overriding guilds' non-members read the 'non-member' string rather than the player's name, and all four gender swaps are pinned in BOTH halves (the rank that swaps AND the string it swaps to). The swap is pinned PER RANK, not per guild: a female at rank 7 of the Dark Brotherhood reads 'Assassin', so moving a swap to a neighbouring rank fails. Five mutations were run against it - the swap made inert, the non-member marker ignored, the marker leaked to members, the rank index off by one, and the female string set equal to the male one - and all five died. |
| guildserviceactions.test.js | 16 | U24's three performable guild services, from DaggerfallGuildServiceTraining/Donation/CureDisease. Training's cooldown is pinned as a DIFFERENCE of 720 classic minutes at both boundaries (719 too soon, 720 clear), and the too-skilled gate as STRICTLY greater - a player sitting exactly on the 50 cap may still train, which an off-by-one would silently take away. `Random.Range(10, 20 + 1)` is pinned at BOTH ends to hold its inclusive upper bound, and the three-hour cost is pinned as the pair DFU writes: 180 minutes of clock and DefaultFatigueLoss x 180 of fatigue, charged in one go because DFU's own per-minute drain fires once across a jump. The donation chance is the pin worth having: `(2 * amount / max(rep,1)) + 1` with C# integer division, pinned at a value where truncating and rounding differ, and reading the ABSOLUTE reputation - a temple that hates you is exactly as hard to impress as one that loves you. The change is pinned NON-propagating against a two-node tree, because a flat one-faction fixture cannot tell propagation apart and a mutation run proved it: the pin passed with propagate:true until the parent was added. Curing is 250 per disease through quality and bargaining with the NEUTRAL regional adjustment (DFU calls CalculateCost with two arguments), only Arkay discounts it and only for members, three holidays cure free even with an empty purse, and the haggle message is pinned as three SHIFT bands at their exact boundaries with an odd cost to expose the truncation |
| guildserviceflow.test.js | 15 | U23's law half - StaticNPCClick's routing and the guild popup's OnPush. The routing pins are the branch table itself plus DFU's TWO bug-numbered escapes, each named in its own source: t=1238 sends a holy-order NPC standing in a non-divine building to TALK rather than to a service menu it has no guild for, and t=2037 does the same for the Thieves Guild spymaster in a faction-less building - with the third TalkToStaticNPC argument pinned as surviving the routing, since it is the only thing that makes that talk different from an ordinary one. Merchants are pinned as routing by BUILDING type and not by the NPC. Setup's member flag is pinned FORCED for the Dark Brotherhood and for GeneralPopulace - which is the Thieves Guild's group, the reason that arm looks mismatched and is not. The two refusals are pinned as DIFFERENT: a non-member is told to join in a plain string, a member of too low a rank gets TEXT.RSC 3100, and the same temple service clears at the rank its column names. OnPush is pinned in DFU's own order - the heal lands BEFORE its box, the recharge AFTER, damaged attributes ASK before curing and only the Yes branch cures, a positive stat mod is not damage - and the rank step is pinned carrying UpdateRank's own text id rather than a recomputed one |
| guildserviceflows.test.js | 17 | U24's list picker and the three message-box chains. DaggerfallListPickerWindow's geometry is pinned against its literals - PICK00I0 at (60,36), the list at (26,27,138,72), the 9x9 paging buttons, rowsDisplayed 9 and rowSpacing 1 - plus the SELECTED row colour, which is DaggerfallUI's 162,36,12 dark red and not the brighter yellow a reader would guess. Paging is pinned moving a WHOLE PAGE and clamping to Count - RowsDisplayed at both ends, and a list click is pinned resolving through the SCROLL INDEX rather than the visible row. The chains are pinned in DFU's own order: training checks gold BEFORE the picker opens (so a poor player never sees the list) and checks the skill cap BEFORE taking payment (so a too-skilled pick costs nothing); the donation field opens pre-filled on "1000", refuses everything but digits, caps at 8 characters, and does NOTHING AT ALL on an unparsable entry, which is what int.TryParse's missing else means; the free-holiday cure fires on OPEN, before any question and with no gold at all. The picker's result box is pinned present after a pick - the live probe found the chain closing on itself and throwing that box away |
| guildservices.test.js | 12 | G3's guild services against Services.cs and the Benefits regions. The service NPC table is pinned as DFU's SWITCH rather than its enum names, by the three entries where the two DISAGREE - MG_BuySpells maps to BuySpellsMages, KO_Smith to ReceiveArmor, KO_Seneschal to ReceiveHouse - which are exactly what deriving from the `PREFIX_ServiceName` suffix gets wrong. Pinned that -1 in the temple service table PASSES the rank gate rather than meaning "never" (CanAccessService tests `<= rank`; the service is simply not OFFERED, since there is no NPC - the gate and the offer are different things, and G2's comment had it backwards). The money formulas are pinned on their TRUNCATED values - a rank-3 reward on 1000 gold is 1296 not 1300, a rank-9 repair 97 not 100 - because the C# `/10` truncates before the multiply and a cleaner formula passes at rank 0 and drifts everywhere else. Plus: training caps at 50 and costs the 100/400 split TIMES LEVEL with no rank discount; a knightly order trains NOTHING; a temple trains NON-MEMBERS where a guild does not; the Mages Guild's free recharge is gated on the Sorcerer's NoRegenSpellPoints flag; a knight rooms free at rank 4 or at any rank in their own region; and only Arkay discounts curing. The temple training-vs-rank split is pinned as MEASURED - five differ, Kynareth/Mara/Zenithar are identical - after a blanket "they always differ" pin failed on three of eight |
| guildservicewindow.test.js | 8 | U23's window geometry and hit routing against DaggerfallGuildServicePopupWindow's #region UI Rects. The panel position is the pin that matters: DFU declares `Position = new Vector2(0, 50)` and then sets BOTH alignments to Center/Middle, which makes BaseScreenComponent ignore it entirely - so the panel sits at (95, 75) and the test asserts it is NOT 50, which is what a reader porting the declared line would have written. The four button rects are deepEqual'd against the literals and their 9px stacking checked. The rest is the window's own machine: each rect routes to its own handler; a MEMBER's join rect is DEAD rather than merely unpainted, since Setup only ADDS that button for a non-member; an OnPush box stack eats clicks until it clears, one per box, and a YesNo box refuses a stray click; the No branch does not run onYes; and a join offering a YesNo closes the popup with the box, because JoinButton_OnMouseClick calls CloseWindow before showing it |
| guildvariants.test.js | 17 | G2's variant-keyed guilds (Temple's eight divines, KnightlyOrder's ten orders) and the join decision. The variant tables ARE faction ids, so most pins run on the real FACTION.TXT: all eight divines are type God with EXACTLY ONE child - their templar order - and all ten orders are ggroup 9. GetDivine is pinned taking either the temple hall's faction or the TEMPLAR ORDER's (resolving through the parent, so the Order of Arkay answers Arkay), and GetOrder is pinned NOT walking a parent, by asking it for an order's parent and requiring null. Per-divine data is pinned as per-divine (different skills, different welcome records) against the orders' one shared list, and the verbatim oddities are pinned rather than tidied: Arkay's blessing id really is 0, Julianos alone welcomes on 6610 and alone sells magic items, and Zenithar's faction is named "Zen". A temple and an order are run through calculateNewRank unchanged, proving the variant record is the shape the G1 rank law already consumes. Last, the join decision: bad-rep and low-skill route to DIFFERENT TEXT.RSC records (a player refused for the wrong reason has been told to fix the wrong thing), and the Thieves Guild and Dark Brotherhood refuse the question outright - both throw NotImplementedException in DFU because they are joined by INVITATION; AUDIT 21 added five, all pin-quality, all against mutations the lane proved survived: F11 all 112 TEMPLE_DATA values (8 rows x 14 columns - four had been pinned to a literal, one column range-checked, and the other 107 asserted only to be OF TYPE NUMBER, so scrambling Mara's library/healing/buyPotions left the suite green); F12 WHICH guild skills, for all eight temples and the shared knightly list, after they had been checked for validity and never for content - guild skills are the rank law's only skill input, so a wrong entry silently caps every member a rank low; F13 the entire Temple promotion mechanism, which had NO pin at all and survived five independent mutations - TEMPLE_PROMOTION's eleven ids, templePromotionId at each selecting rank, and the templeOf -> promotionForRank -> promotionTextId wiring, including Arkay rank 4 where makePotions and soulGems are BOTH 4 and DFU's declaration order decides (reordering TEMPLE_SERVICE_ORDER inverts that one answer and nothing else would notice); F14 TEMPLE_TEXT, KNIGHTLY_TEXT and the knightly promotion map as literals; F15 guildGroup by value on every temple and order plus a corpus check that each record's group is the one the shipped FACTION.TXT carries, since membershipKey IS that value and the pre-existing 'count is 2' pins were satisfied by any group at all. The expected literals were GENERATED from Temple.cs and the subclass files - decoding RankData's positional constructor through its own field assignments, note makeItems -> this.makeMagic - then diffed against the port mechanically, not re-transcribed from what the port already said. U23 added the rank-title tables themselves: ten DISTINCT strings apiece (a duplicate is how a mis-transcribed slot shows up), the male form sitting in the ungendered table where DFU swaps it, and femaleRankTitles keyed by EXACTLY the ranks femaleTitleRanks names - a slot declared but not filled falls silently through to the male string in getTitle, which is the failure this pins against - plus the identity check that all eight temples and all ten orders share ONE table object apiece rather than per-variant copies that could drift |
| holidays.test.js | 7 | S29's FormulaHelper.GetHolidayId. Both tables are pinned at 53 rows with the loop bound covering all of them, which makes the enum's 54th member Old_Life_Festival unreachable - swept over every day of every region to prove it, with the last row Saturalia proved reachable in the same sweep so the pin cannot pass by the loop simply stopping early. The `dayOfYear <= 355` gate is pinned by sweeping days 356-360 across all 62 regions and requiring None, and by showing the tables end exactly at 355. The 0xFF/regionIndex+1 rule is pinned on Scour_Day, which one region celebrates and its neighbours do not. Last, a structural pin: every reachable id equals its table row plus one, checked row by row, so a shifted table renames every festival and fails instead of drifting. U39 added the ACCESSOR pin: the tables are indexed by enum MINUS ONE, and reading one with the enum value lands on the NEXT holiday's row - a silently wrong answer that U39's own first draft shipped, so holidayDayOfYear/holidayRegion exist to make the mistake unavailable and both halves of the offset are pinned (New Life is day 1; row 1 is the next holiday), with every everywhere-celebrated row proven to be found on the day the accessor names |
| iteminfo.test.js | 11 | U25's ItemHelper.GetItemInfo. The thirteen record ids are deepEqual'd, and the branches that pick between them are pinned where they are easy to miss: armor and weapons each have a with- and without-material record, an ARTIFACT never shows its material, a helm and a shield never show one under classic's own setting default, and an arrow has a record with no condition line at all. The condition ladder is pinned band by band at every threshold boundary (`while (pct > threshold[i]) i++`, so 40 is Worn and 41 is Used), including the fall-out case where an item above its maximum prints a raw number. The two computed macros are pinned as DFU writes them - the C# "+0;-0;0" format really does put a plus on every real armour value - and the material name is pinned reading TWO differently-keyed enums, armor's bit-packed against the weapon's plain 0..9. Last, a corpus sweep: nine items through the real TEXT.RSC, asserting NO MACRO SURVIVES to the screen. AUDIT 22 added two: all EIGHT variants of TEXT.RSC 3100 read distinctly through the new variant reader while a single-variant record is untouched and the pick stays in range at both ends (the port drew variant 0 forever where DFU picks at random); and a producer sweep for the three item flags - artifact, azurasStar, oghmaInfinium - that these branches read and NOTHING in src/ writes, pinned BOTH ways so the day one gains a producer its author is sent to the flag |
| mysticism.test.js | 13 | S26's Mysticism, the effect library's one entirely empty school. Pinned first that NOT ONE of the ten supports magnitude - the structural reason the school needed its own module rather than another branch of applySpell's magnitude ladder - and all ten classic keys against the same DFU extraction the coverage measurement uses. The Open/Lock ASYMMETRY is the centre: Open yields a lock only to a caster whose LEVEL REACHES it (equal is enough, and a door it cannot beat stays shut), while Lock has no level test at all and simply imposes the caster's own level - a level-9 caster cannot deepen a level-3 lock, and an already-locked door is refused rather than re-locked. The Skeleton's Key ignores the level rule on INTERIOR doors - X3 pinned the EXTERIOR building arm as a different rule entirely (Open.cs:146-161: no door record, only a building lock VALUE, a strict `Level < value` failure so an equal level clears, and DFU explicit that the Key gets NO exemption out here). Dispel is pinned rolling PER TARGET rather than once for the group, and as DESTROYING rather than killing (DFU: no loot, and it can break quests). Soul Trap fills AZURA'S STAR before any ordinary gem wherever it sits in the pack, and azurasStarOnly refuses to fall back. Silence blocks only a cast that COSTS spell points, so an item cast fires through it. S27 added three HOST SWEEPS: silence gating BOTH the ready and the cast (each clearing the readied spell - the gates now read in hostMagic.js, the S30 engine), the four-host sweep - originally pinning casting as DUNGEON-ONLY so a host growing a cast path would fail it and send its author to the gate, which S30 DID, so the sweep is INVERTED: every host now must mount the ONE engine - and that Open/Lock are still unwired, which fails the moment either ActionSystem owner calls the door payload without the record being updated. These hosts have no execution coverage in node, so the seam is read rather than run |
| trademodes.test.js | 13 | U40's TRADE-MODE LAW (DaggerfallTradeWindow.cs + FormulaHelper's two cost formulas). WindowModes pinned with Inventory INCLUDED, because dropping DFU's "should never get used" member renumbers every mode after it; the action-panel art per mode with Sell and SellMagic proven to SHARE one texture, Inventory proven to answer null (the null guard at :213 is what that is for), and INVE11 proven NOT to be any mode's art - it is loaded in every mode purely as the source of the select button's selected state. CalculateItemIdentifyCost as a SHIFT (a 10-gold item identifies free), free outright on the Witches Festival and on no other holiday, with the guild discount applying AFTER the shift. THE THREE BUY-MODE HOLIDAY ARMS pinned as three different predicates rather than one: Merchants Festival halves everything but only OUTSIDE a guild, Tales and Tallow only INSIDE the Mages Guild (and the faction id is checked, so another guild does not get it), Warriors Festival only weapons and only outside a guild. THE HALVING IS EXACT, and the pin says why: CalculateCost's last line is `2 * (...)`, so every price it returns is even - swept over eight values x six qualities rather than asserted on one case - which makes DFU's integer `/= 2` unable to reach an odd number, and makes halve-per-unit vs halve-the-total an EQUIVALENT MUTANT, recorded rather than re-hunted. SELL DOES NOT PRICE BY CONDITION though its call site says it does: DFU passes ConditionPercentage into CalculateCost's third parameter and the body never reads it, so a battered sword and a pristine one fetch the same price - pinned by equality between the two, and by showing the port's third slot really is the regional adjustment. SellMagic verbatim WITHOUT the stack multiply (DFU's own fencing TODO sits on that line, so five soul gems fence for the price of one). Repair skipping a running job and Identify skipping a known item, each pinned as ONE fact with the button state, because DFU sets modeActionEnabled from inside the arm - a list of nothing but skipped items totals zero AND cannot be committed. GetTradePrice's three-way split with Identify not haggling at all and an unexpected mode THROWING rather than quietly answering the cost. The haggle message proven to BE cureOfferMessageOffset over seven prices (DFU wrote the same three bands twice; the port has one home) plus the +3 that moves both sell modes onto their own records. The buyer refused BEFORE the offer with the two records CONCATENATED and no Yes/No, the seller never gold-checked at all. And the letter of credit: proceeds weighed before they are paid, the boundary inclusive, the letter carrying the FULL price. 23 mutations, 23 dead |

Four laws that do not read the way they look, each pinned: withdrawing gold can be refused for WEIGHT and is refused OUTRIGHT rather than partly paid (a million pieces is 2500 kg), with the boundary inclusive; the letter-of-credit commission leaves the ACCOUNT while the letter is worth the plain amount; the balance test comes BEFORE the minimum, so 50 gold on an empty account is refused for funds rather than for size, and the same request with funds present IS too small; and borrowing credits the account with the amount while debiting the loan with amount + 10%, so the interest exists from the instant the loan is made and repaying early saves nothing. Depositing draws on the WAGON only for the shortfall, emptying the purse first. Repaying spans purse, letters and account in ONE transaction, and an overpayment CLAMPS to the loan and tells the caller what was actually taken. The reminder fires on a CROSSING and not on a state - pinned by showing a tick that stays inside the same month says nothing, which a "months <= 3" poll would not - and the overdue test compares against NOW, so a due date the tick stepped OVER is caught on that tick. An overdue loan raids the account first and defaults ONCE, carrying CRIMES.LoanDefault, which the court law has had since S25 with no caller at all. Last, DeductGoldAmount, which the port had been standing in for with a clamp: a LETTER OF CREDIT is legal tender (whole letters spent first, then the purse, with a part-spent letter written down rather than destroyed), a payment inside the purse never breaks one, and an underpayment RETURNS the shortfall instead of silently succeeding. 19 mutations, 17 dead and 2 recorded as genuine equivalents - splitting the loan-interest truncation cannot be told apart for an integer amount, and DFU's own outer reminder guard is implied by the 6/3/1 predicate inside it. Plus the SAVE round-trip: the accounts, the loan and its due date all survive a snapshot, a different region keeps its own gold and its defaulted flag, the snapshot is DETACHED from the live entity (banking after a save must not rewrite the save - and the pin mutates the ENTITY, because mutating the restored copy proves nothing), and a pre-B1 save restores empty rather than throwing H3 ADDS SHIP OWNERSHIP and the two SELL arms - the last two banking leaves, and both smaller than the ledger claimed: the tables, prices and decisions had all shipped with B1/H1/H2, so what was missing was the ownership STATE, the transactions that move it, and three host producers that were literals (`houseSellPrice: () => 0`, `ownsShip: () => false`, `isPortTown: () => false`). Two of the pins here are deliberately weak and say so: DFU's `ship >= 0` guard is NOT observable in JS (`arr[-1]` is already undefined), and both ship prices are exact multiples of 0.85 so the truncation cannot show on them - the house row is the one that catches a dropped cast. What the ship rows do catch is None falling through to Small's price. Also pinned: a purchase empties the PURSE before the account covers the shortfall, a sale credits the ACCOUNT rather than the purse because a deed is paid into the bank, and both of the ship's scenes - exterior and interior - become permanent on purchase and are dropped on sale. |

| scenecache.test.js | 10 | P1's SCENE CACHE and PERMANENT SET (SerializableStateManager's scene half). The two GetSceneName formats are pinned CHARACTER FOR CHARACTER, because they are the cache KEY and a reformatting is a silent miss rather than an error; the two namespaces are proven unable to collide, and LootContainerTypes is carried whole rather than just the one member the port needs, because a partial enum is how the next reader gets the numbering wrong. RESTORING CONSUMES: the entry is deleted as it is handed back, so re-entering twice without leaving finds nothing the second time - the cache is a hand-off, not a store. The cache DETACHES from the live scene, pinned by mutating and then emptying the live array after caching. Only loot and doors are cached, with an enemy list handed in proven NOT to be carried - DFU's own comment says so, which is why a shop's occupants rebuild every entry. The two clear arms: start=true is the new-character path and empties the permanent SET as well, start=false keeps the permanent scenes' data and drops the rest, and the default is TRUE (DFU's own signature). THE PERMANENT CLEAR STRIPS THE CORPSES and nothing else - a body left in your own house does not survive the world moving on while the chest beside it does - and the strip is proven to happen ONLY at that clear, so a corpse survives stepping outside and survives a save. A permanent scene never entered carries nothing but stays permanent. Last, the whole cache rides the PLAYER SAVE and comes back as a live Map and Set rather than a plain object, with a pre-P1 save restoring empty. And A MODAL OVERLAY OWNS ITS INPUT, from Mac playing the deployed build - the wizard came up over a live dungeon, the map ignored every click and the mouse still swung the view, which are the same fact twice: a LOCKED pointer never reaches the DOM (every mouse event goes to the locked element as a delta, so a fixed div is invisible to it at any z-index) and the hosts only gate RE-taking the lock, nothing released one already held. The view drops the lock and keeps dropping it, its keydown is on CAPTURE and stops there so the host never sees a key the wizard used, and a key the wizard did NOT use still reaches the page. And A REPAINT MUST NOT MOVE THE PAGE UNDER THE PLAYER, from Mac playing on a phone - every tap on a skills stepper threw the list back to the top, which is the hover bug one layer up: these screens rebuild their whole DOM on every state change, and there the destroyed thing was the node under the pointer while here it is the scroll offset. Both enhanced screens repaint through ui/domRepaint.js, and the sweep says so because a repaint written straight takes the bug back and looks like a simplification. THE HELPER'S OWN BEHAVIOUR IS NOT PINNABLE HERE - node has no DOM - and the mutation that makes it record nothing SURVIVES the suite by design; its killer is tools/enhancedScrollProbe.mjs, which taps with a real touchscreen and exits 1 on the mutant (300 -> 0) and 0 on the fix (300 -> 300). That probe took three tries to write and all three failures were the probe: twice it tapped a control scrolled out of view, then it tapped a DECREMENT on a value at its rolled floor, which the law correctly refuses - so nothing moved, the scroll held, and it reported the bug fixed while it was still there. | probehygiene.test.js | 6 | T2 - THE PROBES' OWN HYGIENE, and the only file here whose subject is the tools rather than the port. A probe that lies is worse than no probe, and `tools/tradeModeProbe.mjs` had lied since the day it was written: it booted the exterior host with no `class=`, so the CHARGEN WIZARD mounted and took townTalk's overlay slot, and `townTalk.keydown` - first in that host's keydown ladder (exterior.js:1046-1047) - swallowed every `page.keyboard.press` it made. Its MOUSE surfaces address the window directly and worked fine, which is exactly what made the failure convincing: staging passed, the haggle offer appeared, the Yes never arrived, and it reported "the commit did not charge" against a game that charged correctly. The sweep that found it found FIFTEEN, among them bankProbe typing five digits into a character-creation screen and tavernProbe and guildServiceProbe with seven presses each; the same trap had already cost X11c an afternoon at the other end of the same seam. Rule: a probe that presses keys at a host which mounts the wizard must carry a headless-chargen skip. The ALLOWLIST for the probes that drive the wizard on purpose is self-checking on both sides - a member that grows a `class=` skip has stopped being a chargen probe, and a member that never reads `__chargenFlow` was only ever parked there to silence the rule; either fails. Two of the fifteen turned out to be that first kind and were reverted (audit17mProbe timed out at its own `waitForFunction` the moment the skip was added, which is how they were told apart). The third pin is `tradeModeProbe`'s second lie: 500,000 gold is 1250 kg at `goldPieceWeightInKg`, against a MaxEncumbrance near 90, so every sale it made correctly took ConfirmTrade's letter-of-credit branch while the probe asserted coins - it walks BOTH arms deliberately now and reads the window's own weighing rather than recomputing it. T3 ADDS THREE MORE, on the same disease one step further along: a probe whose EXIT CODE has nothing to do with whether its subject worked. T2 caught `guildServiceProbe` printing OK and exiting 0 on a run where its character had been turned away at the Mages Guild door and no service ran at all. Rule: every browser probe must judge something, and must not print three or more states AFTER its last judgement - that second clause is what catches the guildService shape, whose three exits were all setup guards with ten unjudged prints behind them. EYEBALL TOOLS are exempt (screenshot.mjs, musicRender.mjs and four others whose output is a PNG or a WAV for a human), and that allowlist is self-checking too: a member must actually write a file. THE SURVEY THAT FOUND THE SIX WAS WRONG THREE TIMES FIRST, always accusing working code by missing an idiom - the local `die()`/`fail()` helpers, then the `check()` harness, then the accumulate-and-exit shape `process.exit(ok ? 0 : 1)` that castProbe and firstHourProbe use between them for 45 judgements. The vocabulary is written down in the file so the next sweep does not re-derive it wrong. |

The two grids are pinned as grids: twelve ingredient slots in three columns of four, EIGHT cauldron slots in two, on the 56/38 stride - and the cauldron's slot count is proven equal to the law's capacity, which is what makes the eight-slot cap non-arbitrary. slotAt is pinned to MISS the gaps between buttons as well as to hit them, and the ingredient list's own x offset is proven to apply. Behaviourally: a click moves an ingredient into the pot and out of the list and back again; a FULL pot simply refuses with no message; the pack is filtered so an Arrow never reaches the list. The consume walk is pinned reaching the WAGON, and a BREAK is pinned leaving the pot standing with the first ingredient already gone - a partial spend. The RECIPES button answers a MESSAGE rather than an empty picker when nothing is known, and picking a recipe fills the pot with what the player HAS rather than refusing for one missing herb. Last, the box and picker are proven to swallow input before the window does, so Escape dismisses the box and only the second Escape closes. 8 mutations, 8 dead |
| potions.test.js | 15 | M1's POTION LAW (PotionRecipe.cs, the twenty recipes the effect classes register, and DaggerfallPotionMakerWindow's mixing). THE KEY IS A HASH and the hash is C# INT32: `hash = 17; hash = hash * 23 + id` per ingredient, pinned including the WRAP - nine large ids overflow 2^31 in C# and a naive JS port would grow into a double instead, so Math.imul is the only faithful reproduction and the pin proves the reference really did wrap. An empty list answers 0 and NOT the 17 seed, because DFU returns before the seed is used. The constructor SORTS, so cauldron order does not matter - pinned four ways round - and the RAW hash is proven order-DEPENDENT in the same test, which is what makes the sort load-bearing rather than decorative; sorting is also proven not to mutate the caller's cauldron. The twenty recipes are checked for KEY COLLISIONS rather than assumed distinct (a 32-bit hash over a small ingredient space), every one is reachable through the lookup by its own key, and every one is stored pre-sorted. EVERY INGREDIENT ID is checked against the port's OWN itemTemplates.json - it exists and its template is flagged isIngredient - which is what makes a table transcribed from C# enums trustworthy. Mixing matches by KEY alone with no ingredient comparison anywhere, so a SUBSET and a SUPERSET both fail rather than being close enough, and a failed mix creates NOTHING (DFU's own departure from classic, which makes a useless "Unknown Powers" potion). The cauldron holds EIGHT and purification needs exactly eight, so the cap is proven not arbitrary. The consume walk falls back to the WAGON and BREAKS mid-loop when neither has an ingredient - a partial spend, pinned by showing the first was taken and the third never reached. And the recipe picker fills what it CAN, leaving the rest, with the pool spent down so one herb cannot fill two slots. 13 mutations, 13 dead U44 pins DRINKING one: all twenty rows' effect keys as a table (the transposition to fear is ElementalResistance's enum, where Shock is variant 3 and Poison 2 while the class assigns them Fire/Frost/Shock/Poison), the settings naming ONLY what differs from DefaultEffectSettings' eleven ones with DFU's own name mapping (ChancePlus is chanceMod, MagnitudePlus* are magnitudeLevel*), DrinkPotion's CasterOnly/Magic bundle with purification's three effects SHARING one settings struct, the one effect with no ClassicKey riding under its DFU string key, the magnitudes landing in the real sinks (5+9L healing, 5+19L healTrue, 5+4L magicka with NO x64 where its fatigue sibling has one), and the host sweep - every host hands the drink through its cast engine with BypassSavingThrows | BypassChance. Last the WIRING: all four hosts answer the Rest action, each supplying its own place bag, and PlayerGPS's town-type set is SEVEN types - the port read `locationType <= 2`, which dropped HomeFarms, HomeWealthy, the standalone Tavern and ReligionTemple from every "in town" rule in the codebase, quest gating included. Last, the rest CLOCK: it was named `tickRest` and exactly one host knew to call it, so a rest opened anywhere but a dungeon would have sat on "Hours passed: 0" until Escape - it now rides `tick(dt)`, the seam all four hosts already drive, pinned both that tick advances the clock and that the dungeon host no longer calls BOTH (which would rest at double speed). An adversarial review round added eight more, all for defects it found: THE OPEN GATE (DaggerfallUI.cs:651-687, the dfuiOpenRestWindow handler) is SCENE-FREE and the port had it written out inside dungeonContext because rest was a dungeon feature - the three hosts S40 gave rest to would have let the player lie down with a foe at their back, swimming, or mid-levitation, so it is a law now and all four hosts are pinned to run it BEFORE building the window and to act on its `alert`; and CheckRent (:441-448) was unported, so `remainingHoursRented` was computed by CanRest and dropped - pinned that the rental counts down every rested hour and ends the rest at zero with EndRest's own line (Internal_Strings :358, a STRING not a record, outranking both "You wake up." and "You are healed."), that -1 returns BEFORE the decrement so an unrented rest is never billed, that the counter fires exactly ONCE, that DFU ORs the two finish tests rather than short-circuiting, and that RemoveExpiredRentedRooms runs as the line prints. Plus the capsed-keyboard Y/N and WhileButton's second ButtonClick (:644 then :647, which HealedButton does not repeat). And AreEnemiesNearby (GameManager.cs:684-730) got a home for the same reason the gate did: the two outdoor hosts were asking `activeCount() > 0`, which for rest is a different rule rather than a rough one - guards persist until the crime clears, so one spawned across town blocks sleep forever. Pinned that the RESTING variant SKIPS an unaware foe past 12 units entirely (the whole point of the flag; the strict variant refuses rest for anything in the 1024-unit spawn band), that a foe which can see you counts at any distance, that half-seen is not seen (DFU ANDs Target-is-player with TargetInSight), and that all three foe-bearing hosts ask through the law with the coarse question gone from their rest deps. A second review round added four more, three of them for wires this slice cut with its own hand: createRestDeps destructured a CLOSED option list and returned a CLOSED literal, so the interior host's `onRentExpired` was dropped on the floor while the source-text pin beside it passed - now pinned by building deps THROUGH createRestDeps and driving a refusal end to end, and by asserting the five the composition OWNS win over a same-named key; StartRestGroundedCheck (PlayerMotor.cs:184-194) is NOT the raw grounded flag, and the three new hosts were passing it - the near-ground ray is DFU's own levitation fix, pinned directly on a stub collider for its origin (capsule centre), direction and `height/2 + 0.2` reach; AbortRestForEnemySpawn reached the two slots worldModes owns and not the OUTER host's, which is where an outdoor rest window lives; and worldModes routed the large HUD's panels in EVERY mode including 'exterior', so a REST panel click above ground mounted the interior window into a slot the frame never draws. The round's last find was in its own work: the restVitals pin ran a `career: {}` fixture, and with no RapidHealing flag CalculateHealthRecoveryRate answers the same for all four day/inside combinations - so the pin that existed to prove those flags reach the formula proved nothing. It asks RapidHealing InLight (+100 only outdoors by daylight) and InDarkness (its exact complement) now, so a swapped pair fails from either side. Last, TWO END-TO-END pins, which are the closest thing to a live probe a machine with no ARENA2 data can run: rent a room with three hours left, sleep for nine, and the ROOM runs out first - CanRest passing, MoveToBed landing on the bed the rental minted BY INDEX, the window's own tick driving the clock, three hours of vitals at a rate DERIVED from the formula rather than written down, the expired line outranking "You wake up.", the landlord's sweep firing as it prints, and the same building refusing afterwards; and camping in a city - the confirm box first and touching no crime, the Yes arm committing Vagrancy with guards, no bed in a street, four hours passing, then the same press with the warning OFF refused outright while the crime lands anyway. And PlayerEntity.IsResting, which OnPush raises (:268) with DFU's own comment saying why - "used for random enemy spawning and influences CastWhenHeld durability loss" - and which the port had a live consumer for (enchantments.js picks 60 over 4, a 15x degrade rate) and no feed at all: pinned raised on OPEN before any hour is rested, cleared on all five exits AND on dispose(), with the window asserted to have exactly ONE door that sets `done`, because a flag cleared on four of five exits would leave the player permanently resting for the session. IsLoitering rides the same lifecycle (:789 / :285). Three more from the same lens: ShowStatus (:317-346) picks a different NUMBER per mode - hours PAST for FullRest, hours REMAINING for TimedRest and Loiter - and the port showed hours-past for all three, so a timed rest counted UP where classic counts DOWN; the OnEncounter latch lived on the SESSION, which does not exist while the player is still on the selection page, so a quest CreateFoe wave landing then was simply lost where DFU's window-held flag fires on the first tick after a mode is picked; and endEarly hardcoded "You wake up." for a FullRest where EndRest picks `IsPlayerFullyHealed() ? healed : wakeUp` at the moment it runs. Writing the ShowStatus pin caught the file's third fixture-at-a-boundary: a helper that ticked a LOITER session at the REST cadence (0.75s an hour against loiter's 1.25) advanced it 36 minutes and read the number unchanged. Three more, all from the window's LIFECYCLE rather than its laws: RestFinishedPopup_OnClose is `PopToHUD(); RaiseSkills();` in that order (:728-732), and the port ran them the other way round - every host guards its onLevelUp with "only if the overlay slot is free", and the slot still held the finishing rest window, so a rest-end level-up was SWALLOWED and advancement.js dumped every point into the lowest stats (AUDIT 21 hosts F3's defect, back by another door); RestWindow had no `click`, so townTalk.pointerdown bailed and world.js/exterior.js fell through to requestLook and grabbed pointer lock UNDER the open window, spinning the camera behind the panel; and worldModes' interior seam ticked the window without draining `done`, the one seam of four missing a drain that the other three carry and two call not optional in so many words. And the THIRD reader of IsResting, which the slice's own comment had talked itself out of: PlayerEntity.cs:417-418 is `if (!isResting) DecreaseFatigue(amount);`, so the port was charging 66 fatigue an HOUR through every rest (measured) - and a LOITER, which by DFU's law calls no tickVitals, has nothing restoring it, so a long enough loiter drained the player toward exhaustion while they stood about. Pinned by measurement rather than by regex, and pinned NARROW: the jumping drain (C#:427, outside the per-minute block) must stay ungated and the Swimming tally (C#:414) must stay above the gate, so a mutant that guards both or guards the wrong one fails. Finally a lens that ran its own mutations against the FULL suite found NINE survivors, every one this file's fault, and each is now pinned by running the thing rather than reading it: the interior host's place bag was pinned by regexes over its own source, so flipping `insideBuilding` to false bypassed the whole lodging economy with the suite green (the bag is a law in restSession.js now, and the pin RUNS it); restVitals' three Math.min clamps were exercised by no fixture, and dropping them makes restFullyHealed's `===` unreachable so a Rest-Until-Healed never terminates; `_isPlayerInTownStrict` was unpinned in both outdoor hosts and its anti-regression guard was CASE-MISMATCHED (`locationType()` against the real `_musicLocationType()`) so it could never fire; exterior.js's foe POOL was unpinned, and in that host the watch is the only pool, so an empty one means sleeping through a beating; the fatigue rate was pinned only `> 0`, so any constant passed; the loiter fixture typed 2 against a 3-hour cap, leaving the refusal branch inert; the empty-entry no-op and the 2-digit/99-hour field cap had no test in the repo at all; the gate's refusal MESSAGE was pinned leaving restOpenGate and never arriving at a host, so deleting the lines that show TEXT.RSC 354/355 was silent; and areEnemiesNearby's `_dist ?? Infinity` fallback was never taken. Then two last defects in the code: ALL FOUR of EndRest's arms attach OnClose (:461-:497), the DEATH arm included - DFU sets `youNeverAwaken` and calls EndRest, whose box closes into PopToHUD + RaiseSkills - so the port's death exit was losing a whole night's advancement to a poison that killed the sleeper, while CanRest's refusal (:594-596) is the one arm DFU leaves without an OnClose and must stay silent; and the dungeon was the ONE host of four without the PopToHUD door the previous round gave the other three. A completeness critic then found two more: TickRest's sub-tick is `RaiseTime` AND `QuestMachine.Instance.Tick()` (:376-379), with DFU's own comment saying the ten-minute granularity exists FOR the quest machine - the port took the clock half only, and every host gates its ordinary questBridge.tick on "no overlay up", so a rested night ran ZERO quest ticks (AUDIT 24 wave 30's freeze, other half); and both hours prompts PREFILL the field with "0" and cap it at EIGHT characters (:619/:700, :621/:702), so Enter on an untouched prompt starts a 0-hour rest and the unparseable no-op needs an emptied field - where the port started empty at two digits and called that "the 99-hour cap by construction", making DFU's real TEXT.RSC 26 arm unreachable. Last, the crash the PopToHUD fix itself introduced: RestWindow became the first window in this port to clear the host's overlay slot from INSIDE its own input()/click(), and every host drain re-read the slot afterwards and dereferenced it unguarded (`activeOverlay.done`, `overlay.done`) - so the very key that closes the rest window threw a TypeError in three of the four hosts. Reproduced, then all five drains guarded, and pinned BOTH by driving the shape and by asserting no unguarded drain is left in the tree - plus the same hazard one step further on, where worldModes ticks, drains, and then DRAWS: a tick that cleared the slot left `else if (_shopFont) interiorOverlay.draw(...)` reading null, so dying mid-rest inside a building crashed the frame loop. That seam CAPTURES the window for the tick and drain and then paints whatever is in the slot NOW, which handles an emptied slot and a handed-on one with no branch a test cannot reach. |

The constants as DFU literals - margin 2, MouseOffset (0,4), and both Color32s cross-checked against the SHIPPED settings defaults (404040D2/E6E6C8FF), so the store can be their one home rather than a second copy. Rows split on \\r with the two-character ESCAPED form collapsed first (plain-text files carry the escape). The box size carries DFU's -1, pinned as per-BOX not per-row. The edge handling is a SHIFT, not a flip: a box past the right or bottom edge is pushed back by exactly the overflow and ends flush, never mirrored across the cursor. The delay is a REST - crossing to a different tip restarts the clock, resting on the same one keeps accumulating, an empty text is a hide. Plus the four-host sweep for the hover seam (both overlay channels expose it, every host routes mousemove into one) and the SUPPRESS rule: a key button offers a tip only where its label elongated, and then it carries the full text the '...' stands in for. |

Fully redesigned rather than sprite-traced (Mac's call, per tools/neutral's README), but the ROSTER is not ours to choose: PERSON_TEXTURES is 3 races x 2 genders x 4 archives plus the guard, and an archive with no design spawns a blank townsperson - so the drift guard demands exactly one design per spawnable archive, no duplicates, and race/gender labels that match the table they came from. Every zone's material must be defined (an undefined one falls through to SKIN at build time - a villager wearing their own body colour), every span must be a named RAMPS entry rather than a hand-rolled pair, and designOpts must reverse each span, because the rig indexes ramps by lighting intensity and step 0 is the SHADOW. The palette pins run on the real ART_PAL and caught three of my own bad choices: block 13 is a CLIFF not a ramp (130->100 then a drop to 40), block 11 RESTARTS mid-block, and block 1 is monotone in brightness while alternating gold with neutral grey - so a garment cut from it shades in speckles. That last one needed an OSCILLATION measure, since total hue spread fails to separate it (madder legitimately shifts more), calibrated on the real palette at good <= 17 vs the mixed block's 34. HANGING CLOTH is pinned separately, because it is not a zone: a gown expressed as a cloth zone spanning [body, legL, legR] cannot work - displace pushes a group's faces out from THAT GROUP's centre, so it thickens each leg about its own axis and renders a tunic plus shorts. The render showed it; no numeric check would have. So a skirt, robe or surcoat is a `drape: {name, mat}` naming a pieces/draped.js garment, and the pins demand the name is in DRAPED_NAMES, the material is one of the design's own, designDrape resolves it dark-to-light, and no zone ever spans the torso and the legs again. The editor drives its hairstyle, hair-colour and skin-tone controls off these fields too, so each is pinned against what can actually be built - that is what caught the city watch, whose race 'Guard' is not playable and had no tone, leaving them wearing the previously selected villager's skin. Last, A HOOD LEAVES THE FACE OPEN: gated on height alone it painted the whole skull, so displace grew a depth gate (zLo/zHi, unbounded by default) and the hood became two zones - temples-back plus crown-above-the-brow. The pin measures the BUILD, not the literals: it asks whether any forward, below-brow head face got recoloured, and skips the guard, whose closed helm covers the face on purpose |

| flc.test.js | 14 | F1 - THE FLIC READER (FlcFile.cs), the port's ELEVENTH format reader: Daggerfall's .CEL animations are Autodesk FLICs, a different format from the .VID movies, which is why the U22 video reader could not stand in for the chargen constellations. No ARENA2 in CI, so the decoders are pinned over SYNTHETIC FLIC bytes built to the spec (the book reader's method): the 128-byte header with its SIGNED magic and the 1/1000s frame delay, NumOfFrames+1 frame slots (the extra is the RING frame that loops back), the .FLC/.CEL extension gate; COLOR_256 including the count-byte-0-means-256 quirk and the transparency switch blanking only a matching entry; BYTE_RUN's runs and literals; DELTA_FLC's paired-pixel packets; a PSTAMP thumbnail skipped whole with the chunk after it still decoding; and a bad frame type refusing to play. THE BOTTOM-UP QUIRK is pinned both ways - FLIC row 0 lands in the buffer's LAST row (Unity's texture origin, not the format's) and getFrame() flips it to the top-down shape every other reader here produces. Plus the ARENA2-gated sweep: every .CEL decodes and every frame including the ring frame buffers. AUDIT F added: DELTA_FLC advancing x by PAIRS proven with TWO packets on one line (the single-packet pin could not see the advance at all), an out-of-range pixel write THROWING rather than vanishing (C#'s Color32[] indexer throws where a JS typed array drops the write - the same emulation the VID reader carries), and AUDIT F-P2, which the PLAYER pin caught: C#'s FrameHeader[] is a VALUE-TYPE array, so a slot the frame walk never filled is a DEFAULT struct that decodes as an empty frame and STILL ADVANCES CurrentFrame - the port's defensive refusal instead stalled the counter, so a non-looping player could never reach NumOfFrames and its OnAnimEnd never fired (an animation gating input would have hung for ever). Plus the PLAYER itself (FLCPlayer.cs): Update's order verbatim - the end check FIRST, then the frame clock, then display-the-current-buffer-and-only-then-decode-the-next, so the displayed frame is always the previously decoded one and the ring frame is never shown; the clock gating the first frame; OnAnimEnd raising exactly once; a stopped player displaying nothing; and Loop = true never ending, with start() rewinding. The F2 audit added the ONE-TICK-AT-A-TIME pin: the old 20-tick loop proved only "it ends eventually" and hid both halves of Update's order, so the end check running FIRST (the ring frame never shown, nothing displayed on the ending tick) and display-then-decode are now stepped and asserted per tick |

| tavern.test.js | 9 | U39's TAVERN LAW (DaggerfallTavernWindow.cs + FormulaHelper.CalculateRoomCost). The menu and the price table pinned AGAINST EACH OTHER - the eleven lines carry their prices in the words, so a mismatch is the bug where Stew says 3 and charges 2 - and Heart's Day proven to be the day the holiday table names, through the accessor rather than by eye. CalculateRoomCost as a SPAN and not a "today is": a stay starting on or before day 46 and running PAST it loses one day's charge, one that ENDS exactly on it does not (`+ days > 46` strict), renting only Heart's Day is free, and a long stay starting well before still loses only the ONE day. The rental ceiling counts days ALREADY rented and an EXPIRED room clamps to 0 rather than crediting negative days against it; 300 + 50 is allowed and 300 + 51 is not. The decision ladder in DFU's ORDER, with the load-bearing half pinned as such - the 350-day ceiling is tested BEFORE the knightly exemption, so even a free room cannot be booked past it. Renting mints a record with a bed drawn from the marker COUNT; renewing extends the SAME record; lookup is by map AND building so two inns can be rented at once. GetRemainingHours CEILS, which is what makes the sweep keep a room with one minute left instead of evicting a player up to 59 minutes early, and its null answers DFU's -1 sentinel rather than 0. The hunger gate at exactly four hours. And the two HOLIDAY arms, both of which DFU's own comment flags as not matching their in-game description: New Life skips the gold TEST as well as the charge (so a penniless player eats, and it heals as if bought, because `price` is never re-read), Harvest's End halves with a floor of ONE so a 1-gold ale does not become free - and the halved price is what the healing doubles. Eight mutations, eight dead. P1 added the RETIRED-FLAG pin: renting HOLDS the room's interior in the permanent-scene set so what is left inside outlives the world moving on, a renewal holds it again, and the expiry sweep RELEASES it - the landlord clears the room, which the port had nowhere to say until the set existed. Two more mutations, two dead |

THE PANEL IS CENTRED AND mainPanelRect'S OWN POSITION IS DEAD: DFU sets `Position = mainPanelRect.position` (0, 50) and then sets HorizontalAlignment.Center and VerticalAlignment.Middle, and BaseScreenComponent's alignment switches assign rectangle.x/.y outright on every arm but `None` - so the 50 never reaches the screen and a port that took the rect wholesale would put the box thirty pixels high and hard against the left edge. Pinned both ways: the real (75, 72) and a NOT-equal against the rect's own 0 and 50. Both halves land on .5 and round the way every other centred panel in this port rounds. YES hands the destination back and NO hands nothing back, both closing the box; Escape and E agree with No and Enter agrees with Yes; an unrelated key does nothing at all, which is the mutant that catches a bare `this._no()`. The buttons hit where the rects say and the rects are PANEL-RELATIVE - the same coordinates without the panel offset are on neither button - while the modal swallows every other click; the boundary is pinned at the last pixel in and the first pixel out. A missing TELE00I0 does NOT close the door, unlike the travel map's rethrow: a missing yes/no box must not take the teleport service down with it, so the loader swallows and the window draws a plain rect. And THE HOST ARM KEEPS THE ARRIVAL AND DROPS THE JOURNEY - a source pin, because there is no seam to drive it from: TransitionExterior before the coordinates, the destination climate's weather after, and none of the six things fastTravelTo does around that same call, with the same six asserted PRESENT in fastTravelTo so the list is not a straw man. 6 mutations, 6 dead And THE SERVICE REACHES THE MAP THROUGH A HOST DOOR: the interior arm cannot build this window, because the travel map's dependency list is the world's and only the world host has a streaming world to land in - so it asks `host.openTeleportMap?.()` the way G8's revealLocation is asked, a host without one gets the popup's own "not available yet" arm rather than a crash, and the arm returns the window as WELL as mounting it (an arm that mounts itself and answers null cannot be told apart from a service that does not exist). The world host arms the map before handing it over and refuses without art; the other two hosts are swept to prove they carry no such door. 10 mutations, 10 dead |

| nearbyobjects.test.js | 14 | X4 - PlayerGPS's NEARBY OBJECTS scan, the producer three already-ported laws were waiting on with no feed: the three Detect effects, mysticism's dispelNearby (which had no caller in src/ at all), and enchantments' declared nearbyFoes dep (supplied by ONE of the four hosts). The centre of the file is the GROUP CLASSIFIER, pinned because it is the one part that cannot be guessed: DFU's group bit comes from FormulaHelper.GetEnemyEntityEnemyGroup, a hardcoded CareerIndex switch, and NOT from the port's ENEMY_BASICS `affinity` field, which is a different nine-value classification carrying Darkness/Daylight/Golem/Water that EnemyGroups has no member for - reaching for affinity here would silently misgroup half the bestiary. Pinned per-career: the five places DFU DEPARTS from classic's grouping (Zombie is Undead where classic said animal; Dreugh, Lamia, Horse_Invalid and Dragonling_Alternate all move off classic's undead), the four atronachs taking an EXPLICIT None rather than falling to the default, and a CLASS enemy (a human bandit) being absent from the switch entirely - so it is invisible to a Humanoid scan while a Nymph is not. Then the quirks that decide whether a port over-reports: a CIVILIAN takes Humanoid and NEVER Enemy (DFU's civilian branch is an `else if` that skips the group switch), the MAGIC bit rides effect count alone - DFU's own acknowledged approximation, which is why a buffed friendly lights up Detect Magic - an EMPTY container is not treasure, the query is an ALL-BITS match rather than any-bits (asking Undead|Daedra finds nothing, since no entity carries two group bits), the range test is STRICT where the port's lone existing nearbyFoes used <=, activeInHierarchy is a MATCH rather than a filter so the inactive set is reachable, and the 0.33s rebuild timer RESETS rather than subtracting so a long frame banks no second rebuild. The bridge half runs a real cast end to end: Detect lands as a plain duration buff whose incumbent stacks rounds alone, each spell scans with its own flag, a buffed foe matched by two live detectors yields TWO markers on one bearing (DFU walks detectors outside and objects inside, never de-duplicating), an ENDED entry stops detecting, and the 14-unit reach counts HEIGHT - so the dungeon level overhead does not leak onto the compass. A last pin records TWO DFU DEFECTS this shape avoids: DFU keeps a registeredDetectors list on the compass, so a duplicate cast leaks a registration nothing ever removes, and a Detect spell restored from a save never re-registers and shows nothing for the rest of its duration. The port has no separate registry - activeEffects IS the registry - so a recast merges and a restored entry detects at once |
| soulgemstock.test.js | 9 | X6 - THE SOUL GEM STOCK, the Mages Guild's Buy Soulgems service. X5 made Soul Trap fire and then could not be reached in play: no code path in the port MINTED a soul trap item, so there was never an empty gem for a caught soul to enter. Pinned: the shelf size is DFU's INCLUSIVE loop (`for i = 0; i <= numOfItems`, numOfItems = trunc(quality/2)+1, so trunc(quality/2)+2 gems - the same off-by-one stockShopShelf already honours); the shelf is DETERMINISTIC per GAME DAY and rotates at midnight, because DFU seeds Random.InitState from the day number to stop the stock flickering every time the window opens; the stock is about three quarters EMPTY, since Dice100.FailedRoll(25) is true three times in four and the TRUE branch is the empty gem - which reads backwards until you notice an empty gem is the one a Soul Trap caster actually needs; an empty trap is a flat 5000 written OVER the template's own 500 basePrice; a filled one is 5000 + the creature's SoulPts. Two draw quirks are pinned because a tidier port would lose them: the random soul spans the whole 0..42 monster range with exactly TWO ids vetoed (Dragonling 34, which DFU notes is soulless - only Dragonling_Alternate 40 has a soul - and the unused Horse_Invalid 39), and the veto RE-DRAWS rather than shifting the index, which would silently bias the table; and the eleven creatures whose SoulPts are ZERO are NOT vetoed, so a gem 'filled' with a Zombie is worth exactly what an empty one is. The closing pin is the chain itself: a gem off the shelf must satisfy the very predicate the kill-time fill uses, in shelf order, which is what X5's broken name-based predicate could never have done |
| soultrap.test.js | 10 | X5 - SOUL TRAP, the school's most interesting arm because one of its four outcomes REFUSES the death it was called to resolve. Pinned first that the CAST NEVER FAILS: SoulTrap.ChanceSuccess is hardcoded true (:47-52) purely so the effect always attaches - "chance will be re-rolled when entity is slain" - so a 0.99 roll against a chance of 60 still lands the trap, and the chance is FROZEN on the entry for the kill. BecomeIncumbent's target gate is pinned both ways round, which is the whole point of it: a HUMANOID refusal SPEAKS (DFU's arm `break`s and falls through to the message) while the default arm `return`s first and is SILENT, so a class enemy hears "Trap will not work on humanoids." and the player's own self-cast hears nothing. A recast stacks rounds and cannot sharpen a running trap (the incumbent keeps its own chance), and speaks nothing - BecomeIncumbent fires only for a NEW incumbent. Then the four kill-time outcomes: no trap dies silently, a failed roll dies with "Trap failed.", a passed roll with an empty gem dies with "Trapped soul.", and a passed roll with NO empty gem TETHERS the entity at 1 health and refuses the death. The tether is pinned as NOT a one-off - the effect stays, so blow after blow re-rolls, and the target only dies once a roll fails or a gem frees up: an unfillable trap makes a monster unkillable for the spell's duration. The gem half pins the predicate as GROUP + TEMPLATE, which is what DFU searches by - a name-only impostor and another MiscItem are both refused - after the old default (`name === 'Soul trap'`) was found unable to match any real item, the port's items carrying no `name` and the template's being "Soul Trap"; masked because the function had no caller and its fixtures were built to match the break. Azura's Star takes the soul first and is a REAL identity here (loot.js's createArtifact stamps it), unlike the Skeleton's Key. Finally the gem shows its soul: %hs stops printing "Nothing" over a full trap while an EMPTY one still reads Nothing, and the bracketed-name law keeps DFU's deliberately-blank empty case |
| identify.test.js | 7 | X7 - IDENTIFY: the unidentified-item state, the guild service, and the spell that opens the same window for free. The centre is that IDENTIFIED IS DERIVED, not a field: GetIsIdentified returns TRUE outright for anything unenchanted and only consults the flag for something with magic in it. The port read `item.isIdentified` raw, so a plain iron dagger came back undefined - FALSY - and therefore unidentified; the Identify service would have lit its button over a mundane pack and charged (25 * value) >> 8 to identify a rusty blade. Only the FLAGGED-null destination kept that off the screen, and this lane opens it. Pinned: the derivation both ways (the flag cannot make an unenchanted item unknown, and customEnchantments count as magic too), the mode charging for the unknown and refusing the known, staging following the same derivation, and SPELL mode alone being able to stage an already-known item. The cost is pinned as (25 * value) >> 8 = 97 at value 1000, FREE on the Witches Festival, with the guild hook applying after the shift - though no DFU guild actually overrides ReducedIdentifyCost, so identity is the real behaviour and the hook is a mod seam. The NAMING law hides two things at once: an unidentified item falls back to the bare template name, so an enchanted blade reads "Broadsword" rather than whatever it is called, AND drops its material prefix, so a Daedric one does not announce itself - with an ARTIFACT sharing the material half. The SPELL half pins the per-item pass: an already-known item counts as a SUCCESS rather than being skipped (so the tally is "how many do you now know", not "how many did the spell work on"), a failed roll consumes nothing, and the magicka is spent ONCE for a non-empty list. The cast itself refunds its own cost floored at 5, attaches to nobody, carries the chance rather than rolling it (ChanceFunction.Custom), and lands on a foe not at all |
| knightlygifts.test.js | 8 | G6's KNIGHTLY GIFTS - KnightlyOrder.ReceiveArmor's ladder, the Spymaster's greeting, and the inventory's CHOOSE-ONE mode. THE ARMOUR IS ONCE PER RANK and the bookkeeping is a BITFIELD, not a counter: `ArmorFlagStart << rank` with ArmorFlagStart 4, so the house owns bit 1 and the ten ranks own bits 2..11 - swept for uniqueness and for not colliding with the house bit. A PROMOTION RE-OPENS THE GIFT, which is the whole reason it is a mask, and claiming the new rank leaves the old rank's bit standing. THE MATERIAL IS THE RANK through INTEGER ARITHMETIC on the enum's own values - `ArmorMaterialTypes.Iron + rank`, Iron being 0x0200 with the nine metals above it filling 0x0201..0x0209 - so ten ranks map onto ten metals exactly, and Leather, Chain and Chain2 are UNREACHABLE because they sit below Iron. FOUR TO SEVEN PIECES, NEVER THREE: `for (i = Range(3, 7); i >= 0; i--)` draws 3..6 and runs i+1 times, and the low bound is the one that matters because a straight port of "Range(3,7) pieces" would offer three - pinned at both ends and with a notEqual against the draw itself. The seven body slots are Cuirass..Boots INCLUSIVE, with all four shields swept out and the last index proven reachable. THE GIFT IS CLAIMED BY TAKING: the reward list is the REMOTE side, nothing of the player's can go INTO it, and the take closes the window and fires the callback - so DECLINING COSTS NOTHING and the smith offers again. The flags ride the save, and a pre-G6 membership restores as nothing-claimed rather than everything-claimed. And AN ARM MAY ANSWER A BOX: the smith's refusal and the Spymaster's greeting both do, and the caller used to mount whatever came back - so a box landed in the overlay slot and the next frame asked a plain object to draw itself. The live probe found that; the pin also checks the test comes BEFORE the mount, or it never runs. 7 mutations, 7 dead |
