The group match reuses X4's career table rather than a second transcription, so the two cannot drift. CORRECTED IN X10, and the correction is the most useful thing in this file: these tests previously asserted the arm was a DEPARTURE from a DFU bug, reasoning that both classes act in MagicRound(), neither sets SupportDuration (Charm's is commented out deliberately), SetDuration therefore leaves roundsRemaining = 0, and DoMagicRound's per-round gate only calls MagicRound() when RoundsRemaining > 0 - so the pacify never fires. Every step of that is true except the conclusion. AssignBundle gives EVERY effect one UNCONDITIONAL MagicRound at assign time (EntityEffectManager.cs:594, "At this point effect is ready and gets initial magic round"), outside any duration test, so a duration-less effect fires exactly ONCE at cast - which is the whole design here and matches DFU's own "pacified permanently until player attacks them (confirmed in classic)". The port's BEHAVIOUR was right throughout; the justification was not, and a test pinning the false justification would have kept it alive. These now pin the real mechanism, that the pacify lands at cast with no entry to expire, and that the "until attacked" half is the foe damage doors' existing re-hostile arm |
| dispelmagic.test.js | 8 | X10 - DISPEL MAGIC (6,0), and the BUNDLE TAG the port needed in order to have a picker at all. DFU groups live effects into LiveEffectBundles - one per cast, carrying the spell's NAME and its BundleType - and Dispel Magic removes a whole BUNDLE, not an effect. The port's activeEffects is flat, so a bundle here is the RANGE of entries one applySpell pushed, stamped over exactly the span the E2 heldItem pin already used; the tags ride save/restore free because copyEffectEntry spreads whole entries. Pinned: one cast is one bundle whatever its effect count, a different spell is a different bundle, an equipped-item cast is a HeldMagicItem bundle and IS dispellable (DFU: "confirmed classic allows player to dispel effects from items" - they come back on recast or re-equip, so it is not a permanent strip), an ENDED entry is not live magic, and an UNTAGGED entry belongs to no bundle and is skipped rather than lumped in - inventing one would let the picker offer something it cannot coherently remove. ShowIcon is pinned as per-BUNDLE rather than per-effect: one icon-showing member carries the whole bundle onto the list, and the port's showIcon-false set is the armed Open/Lock markers, the only ShowSpellIcon = false classes that leave a lasting entry. The centre is THE ASYMMETRY: a bundle whose caster is the PLAYER is dispelled UNCONDITIONALLY ("player self-cast spells are always dispelled, otherwise use Chance roll"), so a hopeless roll still strips your own buffs while something cast AT you gets to resist, and a failed roll consumes nothing. Last, the cast itself opens the picker, lands nothing, refuses a foe target, and does NOT refund - DFU is explicit that the cost is charged even if the popup is cancelled, unlike Identify. ONE MODELLING DIFFERENCE is recorded rather than hidden: DFU adds a bundle even when its effect merges into an incumbent, so two Levitate casts show two entries in DFU's picker and one here |
| dispel.test.js | 6 | X9 - DISPEL UNDEAD (6,1) and DISPEL DAEDRA (6,2), the two CREATURE dispels. Both halves were already in the tree and had never met: mysticism.dispelNearby carried the destroy law from an early pass with NO caller in src/ at all, and X4 built the nearby scan the effects read. This is the join. Pinned: ChanceFunction.Custom, so the cast NEVER fails and the chance travels to the per-object rolls - a 0.99 roll against 70 still lands the sweep - and the effect is self-targeted, attaching nothing to the caster and acting once; the sweep taking ONLY the matching group (a daedra and a rat survive a Dispel Undead) and rolling PER TARGET rather than once for the group; and the reach being the scan's own 14 units rather than a spell setting, because TargetFlags_Self means the spell sweeps around the caster rather than aiming. The load-bearing pin is DESTROYED, NOT KILLED: DFU's own comment says the target "is not killed and will drop no loot" and that this "can break quests if used carelessly", so both host arms are read to confirm they route through removeFoe - the Destroy(gameObject) primitive that frees the batch and uncouples the quest resource - and NOT through damageFoe, which would spawn a corpse and roll loot. The last pin covers the one departure the join needed: the port keeps its nearby list warm only while a Detect spell runs and rebuilds it on a 0.33s timer, so a dispel cast with no detector up would read an empty or stale list; scanNow bypasses both the gate and the cadence, which is the honest equivalent of DFU always having a warm list |

The seams: L/U/S/D/E as DialogShortcuts writes them and B as the BUY hotkey that does nothing in the player's own book, Enter readying with the free-cast rider and doing NOTHING in buy mode (only OnMouseDoubleClick is wired there), the CAST binding toggling the book closed, a pushed box swallowing everything but its answers, a click-anywhere box dismissing without closing the book, and every button hit through the half-pixel offset. BUY MODE: the offer drops the '!'-prefixed internals and sorts by name, nothing desaturates (DFU passes null available points), the price is the casting cost x4 halved by a SHIFT on Witches Festival with a floor of one and run through CalculateTradePrice, the ladder is spellbook then gold then the 260/261/262 haggle line with %a and %pct expanded and the band DRIVEN across five shop qualities against cureOfferMessageOffset's one home (plus a source pin that the window did not re-implement the comparison), a LETTER OF CREDIT is tender at the counter and is really SPENT (letters before coins, DeductGoldAmount's order), Yes deducts and adds a COPY and closes while No closes too, and the cast-mode buttons do not exist. Plus the four-hosts sweep (every host imports the classic window, builds it in ONE place, hands the player's OWN array by reference and warms the art) and the two BuySpells guild arms landing on one destination in buy mode, the STRINGS pinned as literals against DFU's own en table (Internal_Strings.csv) rather than through the constants, the scroll clause living INSIDE the movement guard and nudging by one (wheel away, then press an arrow at an edge), the SOUNDS per mode with arming a prompt SILENT and the arrow buttons not, plus the three icon TOOLTIPS (the static selectIcon on the spell panel, the target and element descriptions following the SELECTION, nothing off-panel or behind a pushed box) and the `__spellbook` probe surface on both exterior hosts and castProbe's reopen-before-ready (the keyed window stayed open through its sort confirmation; the classic one does not). 103 mutations across two campaigns, 102 dead and one proven equivalent; the second campaign ran only the laws the adversarial review corrected and started with 14 alive |

| infection.test.js | 16 | V1 - THE INFECTION HALF: VampirismInfection and LycanthropyInfection, the two diseases that end by replacing the player's race. THEY ARE DISEASES, which is the whole trick: DiseaseData of all zeroes at 0xFF days ("Permanent no-effect disease, will manage custom lifecycle"), so the daily tick, the temple's count and Cure Disease all carry them while the effect counts its own days - pinned by driving updateDiseases nine days over an infection and finding zero statMods, no countdown and no incubation flag, because UpdateDisease is OVERRIDDEN and does not call base (without that arm the walk reads DISEASE_DATA[null] and throws on the first day). THE TWO GATES ARE BOTH OFF BY ONE against DFU's own comment: `daysPast > 0` for the warning dream (the day of the bite is silent) and `daysPast > 3` for the turn (the FOURTH day, not the third), and the second ALSO requires the dream to have PLAYED, not merely been scheduled - the flag comes off the modal video's OnClose, so a dream left open holds the infection at that gate for ever, pinned a year out. THE TWO DEPLOYMENTS DIFFER IN WHO FIRES THEM: lycanthropy turns in the tick, vampirism schedules a fake DEATH there and turns on ITS close, so a vampire whose death video is already pushed gets 'idle' from the tick however long they wait. THE CANCELS: a racial override cancels an incoming infection outright, the same strain twice ends the DUPLICATE so the clock is not restarted, and the two lycanthropy strains cancel each other - the OPPOSING one ONLY, so a player can be four days from two different fates at once (a `live.length` test bars the vampire bite and dies here). GetVampireClan DEFAULTS TO LYREZI, not None: the enum HAS a None the function never returns, and a region with no Province record still turns you - pinned because None is the reading a person gives the switch. The clan is read from where the bite happened, at the moment of the turn. THE CLOCK RAISE IS SIGNED - `(2*SecondsPerWeek) + (DuskHour + 1 - Hour) * 3600` - so turning at 22:00 lands three hours BEFORE the fortnight rather than after it, and all 24 start hours are walked to prove every turn wakes at 19:00 (an abs() or a clamp passes a "roughly two weeks" pin and dies here). AND THE TEMPLE'S PRICE IS ALREADY RIGHT, which corrects the Ledger row that opened this arc: an infection counts alongside an ordinary disease, and EndDisease is the turn's LAST line, so a cure bought a minute before the turn works and a minute after has nothing to cure. Plus the pass itself - each video PUSHED once and the lifecycle carried on its close, the null-object host that still reaches the turn, the registered host supplying the clock and the popup, and the whole thing driven through worldTick's magic round, which is its ONE home. AND THE SAVE, both halves: the infection rides activeEffects like any disease (with the region it was caught in, which the clan is read from at the turn), but the moment it DEPLOYS the disease is over and the marker is the only record left - a save there used to come back human, and catchable. 18 mutations, 18 killed |

THE SETTING NOTHING READ: `GUI/LargeHUD` has been in the store since the MENU slice and the pause window's FULL SCREEN button has been WRITING it since I3, with nothing on the other end - the settings screen was reporting a working toggle for a bar that did not exist. IT IS AN ALTERNATIVE HUD, not an addition: DaggerfallHUD.cs:214-220 turns off the vitals, the compass AND the interaction-mode icon while it is up, and the CROSSHAIR survives - pinned by slicing the branch out of drawHud and reading what is inside it. ELEVEN CLICKABLE PANELS, EIGHT OF WHICH DRAW NOTHING (the bar art has the buttons painted on), proven DISJOINT in both directions so DFU's component order cannot matter, with the head clickable and the three vitals beside it on the same colour field NOT. THE TWO MODE CYCLES DISAGREE, and it is DFU's own: PlayerActivate.NextInteractionMode walks the enum (Steal > Grab > Info > Talk) and the panel walks Steal > TALK > GRAB > Info, so clicking the bar's mode button and pressing the mode key move through the same four modes in different orders - reusing the keyboard's cycle passes any "it cycles" pin and dies here. The right click is the exact inverse of the panel's own, round-tripped from every mode. DOCKED collapses AutoSizeModes.ScaleToFit's arithmetic to the screen's full width in proportion, flush to the bottom (an integer-floored scale leaves a gap and fails); UNDOCKED is the native scale times LargeHUDUndockedScale with alignment None FORCED to Centre, so 0 and 2 are the same bar. The compass frame is a C# `(int)` TRUNCATION over 32 frames, walked at 401 headings to prove nothing indexes past the last, with the port's wrap covering the 1.0 DFU's own read can never produce. Only the MAP panel differs on the right button (the automap left, the travel map right); every other panel binds ONE handler to both, which is checked from the other side. And every panel posts an ACTION ui/input.js routes - the same door the keyboard uses, driven through a recording ctx - with the five arms U45 added reporting honestly when no host has grown a door and the four that predate it returning true regardless, pinned as the inconsistency it is. Plus the four-hosts wiring: all four DRAW the bar, and the four that own a pointer offer it the click and bind ActivateCursor. 11 mutations, 11 killed |
| bss.test.js | 4 | U45 - THE BSS READER, the twelfth image format and the simplest file in ARENA2: a ten-byte header of five Int16 (XPos, YPos, Width, Height, FrameCount) and then FrameCount frames of raw palette indices back to back, so `10 + n*w*h` IS the file size. That is the corpus gate, and all three shipping files pass it to the byte. ALL THREE CARRY 32 FRAMES and differ in SIZE - 48x40, 34x28, 30x25 - so a reader that hardcoded the standard needle's dimensions would decode two of the three as garbage while consuming exactly the right number of bytes; both come from the header. The header's own screen position (272, 157) is read and never used, because HUDLarge places the compass at its own (275, 2) inside the bar - ported because it is in the file and a reader that silently drops two fields cannot be checked against the bytes. The refusals are DFU's: a wrong extension returns false rather than throwing, a truncated body is a load failure that leaves no record behind, and an invalid record answers -1 frames rather than 0. RECORDED EQUIVALENT: dropping the frame upper-bound guard survives every pin, and genuinely is equivalent - C# throws IndexOutOfRange on a Color32[] where JavaScript answers undefined, which `?? emptyBitmap()` turns into the same empty bitmap. 3 mutations, 2 killed, 1 recorded equivalent |

| orcbody.test.js | 14 | The rig's BUILD girth spec + the orc line. The load-bearing pin is the INERTNESS of opts.build: no opts / {} / BUILD_IDENTITY all return a byte-identical face list, because raceCharacter, engineRig and villagerRender call the rig with no build and the villager payload is packed as a DELTA against that exact list - a silent shift would corrupt 25 villagers and every race body at once. Plus: a partial build keeps the unnamed keys at identity, every design changes geometry WITHOUT changing the face count (the delta packing depends on it), out-of-band and junk values clamp to 0.6..1.6 or fall back to identity rather than tear a seam, every shipped build value sits inside that band, the four designs' level/damage/team are pinned against their ENEMY_BASICS rows (7/12/21/24) so a table regen cannot leave a silhouette tuned to a stat the enemy lost, the melee three escalate monotonically while the shaman (chanceForAttack2 20, the caster) sits deliberately off that curve with the longest tusks, orcOpts resolves every named material dark->light out of ART_PAL with no zone referencing an undefined one, and the tusk/brow pieces are finite, quad, head-tagged and shaded - a bigger tusk grows LONGER from the same socket rather than walking down the chin, and both track the jaw they anchor to |
| undeadbody.test.js | 20 | The undead line (Zombie, Mummy) on the mechanism the orc line proved. The load-bearing pin is that every BUILD key a design declares is one the rig ACTUALLY HAS: BUILD_IDENTITY is torso/shoulder/arm/hand/neck/skull/jaw/leg, the rig spreads a spec over its own keys, and an invented one is dropped in silence. `gut: 1.24` on the zombie would have shipped a corpse with a flat stomach and nothing anywhere to say why - it is a displace zone now, and the swell is asserted rather than assumed. Also: zone materials are declared, and both ride the base face list so they can ship as deltas. THE SKELETON is the third design and the first that needed GEOMETRY: ribs are separate bones with gaps between them and no girth multiplier will ever put a hole in a closed loft, so its silhouette comes from a piece (pieces/skeletonBones.js) while the body is scaled away under it. Pinned: only it carries bones, its torso and neck are below 0.7 or it is a man in a rib costume, the cage is six hoops and a sternum rather than a solid barrel, the pelvis is two blades with a gap, and every bone is tagged 'body' so it rides the torso through a swing instead of hanging in the air. THE LICH is the composition test: every enemy before it carried ONE kind of thing (a tusk, a cage, a body) and a lich is a skeleton IN ROBES, needing the piece table and the drape path to share a figure when they had never been asked to. Pinned: it carries both, its robe material is declared, and the drape survives undeadOpts — without which the viewer never sees it. The bones check that read "only the skeleton" failed correctly when the lich arrived and now says what is actually true: bones go to the bone-bodied, and nothing with flesh on it gets a ribcage — and that check now states the RULE rather than listing its members, having broken twice (once for the lich, once for the ancient lich) while only keeping a register. THE VAMPIRES are the first designs here that keep their body, so they say everything with COLOUR: pinned that the pallor reads at least 1.8x the coat, or the whole figure is a silhouette and the design has said nothing, and that their build stays inside 0.85-1.2 because a body they kept is a body. THE DRAPE FIT: a garment is carved ONCE per name and shared by every design that wears it, which is what keeps the payload small — but the body under it is resized by its build spec and nothing was telling the cloth, so every drape sat at scale 1.000 whoever wore it and the wearer came through. Pinned: the fit exceeds the wearer's torso, armour under a garment pushes it further out (two designs of equal girth and unequal plate must not get the same fit), and the scale is GIRTH ONLY - the check reads the actual scale.set call and fails if Y is ever touched, because every exported constant on this rig is a height and scaling a garment vertically drops its hem through the floor on a giant. SPECTRAL (Ghost, Wraith) is the first design here that is not a SHAPE: every enemy before them said what it was with geometry and colour, and a ghost has nothing to add to the figure and everything to take away - what makes it one is that you see through it, which is a MATERIAL property the character path had never been asked for. Pinned: opacity stays between 0.15 and 0.7 (below is a smear, above is not see-through), a wraith is fainter AND thinner than a ghost or the two are the same enemy in different blue, and - the one that bites - the transparency does not OUTLIVE the ghost: the body material is shared by every design, so setting it for a ghost and forgetting to clear it makes the next orc you pick a see-through orc. |
| humanclasses.test.js | 10 | The human class enemies - Mage, Sorcerer, Battlemage, Bard, Thief - collectively the commonest thing in a dungeon (mapChance 3,3,2,2,2) and the last large group with no rig. The load-bearing pin is that they SCALE: every other design carries a level and a damage band out of ENEMY_BASICS and these carry NEITHER, because Daggerfall builds them to whoever walks in - a made-up number would be a lie about the game's own rules, so `null` is the assertion and the viewer prints "scales". Also pinned: the BATTLEMAGE's robe clears his plate, and a mage in the same body without plate gets a TIGHTER fit or the clearance term is not reading the armour at all. He was built after the drape-fit change, which is the only honest way to check it holds for a design it was not written against. THE FILE NOW CARRIES NINETEEN, every human the player meets plus the six that only come by quest, and the added pins are about them being NINETEEN PEOPLE rather than one person nineteen times: no two designs may share a silhouette-plus-materials-plus-drape signature (which caught Assassin and Nightblade shipping as literally the same figure under two names), the torso spread must exceed 0.25 or the cloth is doing all the work, and plate must broaden its wearer more than robes do or the armour is paint. The "is it a person" band was widened from 0.9-1.15 to 0.85-1.32 with cause: it had been drawn around the first five designs, which were all scholars, and a man in full plate IS broader than one in robes. |
| atronachs.test.js | 6 | Fire, Ice, Iron and Flesh - the only group in ENEMY_BASICS whose members are STATISTICALLY IDENTICAL (all four level 16, 5-15 damage, 25-130 health, armour 6). The game tells them apart by element and by nothing else, so this is a design problem with the numbers taken away and the pins are all about difference: no two may share a silhouette, iron outweighs flesh which outweighs flame, and THREE RENDER MODES must survive across four designs - fire ADDS light to what is behind it (additive), ice subtracts itself from it (transparent, and denser than a ghost or it IS a blue ghost), iron and flesh do neither. Plus the same trap the ghost opened: one material is shared by every design in the editor, so the additive blend must not OUTLIVE the fire or the next orc glows. |
| roster.test.js | 6 | THE ROSTER, ENFORCED. Every enemy in ENEMY_BASICS has a voxel design, with one deliberate exception, and this exists so that "which enemies are built" is a question the SUITE answers rather than one somebody has to count - adding an enemy to the game without a body is a failure now rather than a thing nobody notices. The exception is Horse_Invalid: no level, no damage band, no health, affinity None. It is a hole in Daggerfall's own table rather than a creature, and building a body for it would be inventing an enemy the game does not have - so a second test asserts it STILL has no stats, because the moment it does the exclusion stops being honest. Also pinned: no enemy is built twice by two lines, and every design names a real MobileType. Plus the DRAPE SELECTION, which is the third time "a design wears what its design says and nothing else" has had to be enforced somewhere: first the loose armour pieces that stayed on when a villager was picked, then the drape FIT that sized a garment for the wrong body, and now the selection itself. applyVillagerDrape(null) means "no design is selected, leave the control where the user put it" - right for the bare rig, where cycling garments is the point, and absurd for an enemy. Handing it null for every design WITHOUT a drape meant a bat wore whatever happened to be showing: cycle to a skirt, pick an animal, and the animal is in a skirt. The design is passed now so it can say NONE, and a second test asserts the nine animals ask for none - if somebody ever gives a slaughterfish a gown they have to mean it. |
| beasts.test.js | 17 | Giant Rat, Grizzly Bear, Sabertooth Tiger - the first enemies with NO HUMAN IN THEM. Every other design is a person underneath (scaled, stripped, robed, or with a horse hung off him); these collapse the rig's WHOLE body, all six groups, using the mechanism the centaur needed for two, and what the player sees is entirely the piece. Pinned: every group is collapsed or a human arm hangs inside a bear; each animal stands ON the ground rather than through it or above it; one builder produces three animals that are provably not each other (the rat lower, the bear broader, the cat longer) or the parameters are decoration; each has its own pelt because three brown animals is one animal; and - the one that bit - THE PIECE MUST EXIST. Adding 'beast' to PIECE_KINDS without building a table for the line left all three rendering as NOTHING, because the rig under them is collapsed and there was not even a man left to see. Piece tables are keyed by line now. THE WEREBEASTS then split this file in two: a Daggerfall werewolf is NOT a wolf on four legs, it is a man with the wrong head, so it collapses ONE group of six where a full beast collapses all of them. The "every group is collapsed" pin failed correctly when they arrived and now states the rule instead of the roster - collapse exactly what the piece replaces. Plus a CONTRAST pin, the second time contrast has bitten: the wereboar's bristle resolved to a mean of 58 against a background of about 20 and read NARROWER than a werewolf despite being measurably wider, because its edges went into the dark. THE CONTRAST FLOOR THEN MOVED FROM 70 TO 95, because 70 was set by the thing that FAILED rather than by the thing that has to pass: a spider at mean 100 cleared it and was still invisible, being a small body on thin legs with a fraction of a wereboar's area to be seen with. Area and brightness trade off, and the raised floor immediately caught the rat and the grizzly too — two animals shipped a commit earlier that nobody had looked at. ARACHNIDS are the third body plan in this file and get their own builder: the quadruped's assumes a BACKBONE with a leg near each corner, and a spider's legs radiate from one joint in a ring with a body of two lumps rather than a tube. The test of whether a mechanism is right is what happens to the thing that does not fit it — the beasts stretched from a rat to a bear on numbers alone, an arachnid does not stretch at all, and that is the mechanism telling the truth about its edges rather than being forced past them. WINGS are the last body plan: `behaviour: Flying` is the one category in ENEMY_BASICS that is neither foot nor fin, and a wing is a MEMBRANE ON FINGERS - what the eye reads is the sheet between the bones, so it is panels rather than boxes. Pinned: FOLD moves the wing BEHIND the animal rather than shrinking it (a folded bat is narrower AND deeper, and the face count must not change, or it is two pieces pretending to be one), a GIANT bat is sized like a giant one (the first cut used a real bat's proportions and made a flat strip at ankle height that the viewer's own UI sat on), and the same piece serves an imp - a small man who happens to have a pair - at a fold nothing like a bat's. The collapse check has now been rewritten THREE times for the same reason: each version described the kinds of design it knew, and the next design was a kind I had not thought of. It states the rule now - a group is collapsed exactly when something replaces it - and the imp, which collapses nothing, is the case that finally forced it - then the LAMIA became the fifth counterexample, replacing her LEGS with a fish tail, and the rule generalised properly at last: the expectation is whatever a design says it replaces. WATER is the last plan the builders could not say. A quadruped tapers nose to rump and stands on four legs; a fish tapers the same way and stands on NOTHING, which is not a leg length of zero because that leaves a barrel on the floor. One builder gives a whole slaughterfish and a lamia's tail - its `from` is why, since a tail starting at a waist is the same geometry as a fish starting at a head. Pinned: the tail meets the waist (at 0.72 it hung below the collapsed pelvis with daylight between, two animals in a stack), a lamia's SKIN is not her scales, and the dreugh's borrowed claws come without the arachnid's eight legs - legPairs 0, after it shipped standing on its own legs with a spider's underneath. The shared-pelt pin also learned to distinguish a whole fish from a tail: the same scales on two bodies is correct, two brown quadrupeds is one animal twice. |
| beastattack.test.js | 9 | ATTACKS FOR BODIES WITH NO ARMS. enemyAttack.js is a verbatim DFU port and its TIMING is right for everything - a bear and a knight attack on the same clock - but its own comment says it plays "the authored 1H strike clips", which are ARM animations. Nine enemies collapse their arm groups to a POINT: the timer fired, the hit landed, the damage applied, and the bear did not move. For these the motion belongs to the whole animal, because there are no joints to turn. Pinned: every armless design says HOW it reaches you and a design WITH arms does not get one (the lamia has a fish tail and a woman's arms, so she swings); the motion peaks within 0.08 of the frame the game's own primaryAttackAnimFrames marks with -1, so the timing is read rather than invented; a slaughterfish bites TWICE because it carries two -1 frames and nothing else does; the five kinds are provably different curves and a sting goes BACKWARD as it strikes, because the tail does the reaching; and every clip returns to rest - the gather held at full through the recovery and left a lunging animal 0.08 behind where it started, which after three attacks is standing somewhere else. THE STING then came OUT of the body: welded into buildArachnid, the only way to animate it was to move the whole scorpion, so a sting looked like the animal rocking backwards and the one part of it anybody watches did nothing. It ships as five POSES from coiled to thrown - cheaper than a skeleton for one appendage, and how the sprites this port replaces did it - and the attack clock picks one. Pinned: coiled stands UP over the back and thrown comes DOWN and forward, buildArachnid must NOT build it again (if it does the animation dies silently, because the body would still move and nobody would notice the tail had stopped), and only the scorpion has one. |

| drapeclip.test.js | 3 | NO DRAPE CLIPS THROUGH A BODY PART. A garment is an ellipse of rx by rz at each row; a body point is inside it when (x/rx)^2 + (z/rz)^2 < 1. This walks every drape-wearing design, every row of its garment and every body vertex near that row's height. THE FIT USED TO BE GUESSED from torso and shoulder, and that guess cannot see the thing that decides the answer - ARMS HANG OUTSIDE THE TORSO. When the check was first written it found 669 clipping rows and every single one was an arm: a lich's stuck 111% beyond its own robe, because the guess cut that robe at 0.657 where the body needed 1.404. The fit is MEASURED now, at build time where the body and the garment grid are both in hand, and per ROW rather than as one number - a single scale is set by the arms and then applied to the hem and collar too, which turns a robe into a cone. The viewer still applies the widest row uniformly because every garment is VERLET CLOTH and its constraints hold the rest shape, so genuinely widening one means rebuilding it; the per-row data ships and waits for that. |
| paperdoll.test.js | 3 | 27-slot EquipSlots verbatim, 288-template DB (index resolution, Short Shirt pin), BlitItems order |
| dyes.test.js | 3 | Clothing dye range shifts (Blue identity, Red 0xEF), 11 metal tables extraction-pinned, band-only swap |
| enchanting.test.js | 9 | M3's ENCHANTING LAW (DaggerfallItemMakerWindow's cost accounting + FormulaHelper's enchantment-power formulas). E2 had ported what an enchantment DOES; this is what one COSTS. TWO SUMS OVER OVERLAPPING SETS, which is the thing to get right and is pinned as two different walks rather than one sum at two scales: the ENCHANTMENT cost adds BOTH lists and EXCLUDES forced enchantments, while the GOLD cost adds the POWERS ONLY, INCLUDES forced, and multiplies by ten - so a side effect costs points and no gold, and a forced enchantment costs gold and no points, both asserted directly. The two material tables are pinned as tables and not as an index: the progression is NOT monotonic in material order (Silver and Adamantium share +75%, Elven and Mithril share +25%, and silver beats dwarven despite coming first), and armor's base set is BIGGER than weapons' - Leather, Chain, Chain2 and Steel all sit where weapons have only Steel. An unknown material falls to the BASE and never to zero power, because DFU's `default:` shares the Steel arm. THE POWER FORMULA FLOORS, and a negative multiplier rounds AWAY from zero, so an iron item loses slightly MORE than a quarter - pinned against the MODULE over real templates (a Wakizashi's 450 points and Greaves' 50, both chosen because they are not multiples of four) rather than against a local helper, which would only assert the test's own arithmetic back at itself. The comparison is against the MUTANT'S own form too: JS rounds half up, so `base - round(base * 0.25)` agrees with flooring and proves nothing, while `base + round(base * -0.25)` is the swap that actually differs. The GOLD is checked FIRST, pinned with a player who can afford neither - they are told about the gold. And ten enchantments is a CAP rather than a refusal (the first ten apply, the rest drop silently) while an EMPTY list throws. 10 mutations, 10 dead M4 ADDED THREE PINS and corrected one framing. THE SIGN IS THE MECHANIC: M3's own header said a side effect "costs enchantment points", and it does not - every side effect's EnchantCost is NEGATIVE, so summing it REDUCES the total and taking a drawback BUYS budget. Pinned against the CATALOGUE's real costs rather than invented ones, so the two modules cannot drift: a Regens Health power alone needs 4000 points, an item with 450 refuses it, and adding User Takes Damage in sunlight buys the room AT THE SAME GOLD - the overLimit arm quoting no gold at all. `-` for `+` in totalEnchantmentCost is the mutant and it dies. Every side effect in the catalogue is swept to prove none of them is a positive charge dressed as a drawback. THE ENCHANT LADDER HAS FIVE ARMS, not three: no item selected outranks everything (including having no gold), and nothing-prepared outranks the gold check - which it has to, because an empty powers list costs zero gold and would otherwise always pass. That last ordering is an EQUIVALENT MUTANT for exactly that reason and is recorded rather than chased. A side effect ALONE counts as prepared. And THE PICKER GUARD TESTS `== 10`, not `>= 10`: eleven walks straight past it, which is reachable because the room check runs only for a bound soul - so an item can be loaded past the cap and SetEnchantments' silent truncation is what finally drops the surplus. 15 mutations, 14 dead, 1 recorded equivalent |

FOUR SHAPES, and ClassicParam does not mean the same thing in each: a param table indexed 0..n-1; a SINGLE cost minted at ClassicParam **-1**, where a flat `costs[param]` lookup gets six effects wrong and reads 0 as a real param; EnhancesSkill's one FLAT cost over all thirty-five skills, where the param is the SKILL id; and the three CastWhen*, keyed by classic SPELL id - so Ice Storm prices 1420 cast-on-use and 840 cast-on-strike, which an index lookup could never see. A non-integer param answers null, because `costs['length']` would otherwise answer the array's LENGTH and read as a price. The MIRRORS are pinned (PotentVs/LowDamageVs and GoodRepWith/BadRepWith are exact sign mirrors), along with three tables that are not what you would guess: "All" is the sixth reputation param but prices as FIVE groups; BadReactionsFrom is non-monotonic (Animals cheap, the two ends equal); RegensHealth charges the same for sunlight and darkness. SoulBound is 43 rows keyed by monster ID, its names read from enemyBasics.js - so the picker shows TWO rows both named "Dragonling", one worth nothing and one worth 5000. THE SIGN CLASSIFICATION partitions all twenty-four with none both and none neither, and SoulBound alone mixes zeros among its negatives (which is why the classifier tests `<= 0` with a `some(< 0)`). SOULBOUND IS THE ONLY SOURCE OF FORCED ENCHANTMENTS IN THE GAME - swept over every type at every param, not sampled - and nine of the forty-three souls carry a set. Forced children are split by their OWN instance cost at their OWN param and marked with the parent's key; DFU splits on `> 0` while the catalogue classifies per effect, and the two cannot disagree on the nine sets as shipped because no forced child prices at zero - swept rather than claimed, so a zero-cost child added later trips it. THE ROOM CHECK RUNS ONLY FOR A BOUND SOUL: twenty enchantments already held and a plain pick still says add, while the Daedra Seducer's five forced children refuse at exactly 5+5+1. Removing a PARENT takes its children from both lists; removing a child takes nothing. THE MAGIC DEFENCES (X1c): Elemental Resistance dropping a matching-element effect WHOLE through the saving throw's first gate (not scaling it) per element, with a like-kind recast stacking rounds while the incumbent keeps its own chance (AddState touches RoundsRemaining alone) and a different element taking its own instance; Spell Absorption's effect arm swallowing the spell and crediting its recomputed casting cost as magicka, and refusing ALL-OR-NOTHING when the free magicka cannot cover the whole cost; Spell Resistance dropping the effect silently with no magicka gained, never on a CasterOnly cast; and Spell Reflection pinned as still-pending its re-target path (~~pending~~ X11a landed it; the pin now asserts all EIGHT of that row live, and the durable inert example moved to Morph Self). SHIELD (X1d): the magnitude IS the pool one-for-one, absorbed ALL-OR-OVERFLOW per hit (a hit within the pool reduced to zero, the hit that empties it passing only its excess), exactly zeroing it still BUSTING it while passing zero through, a busted pool absorbing nothing further, a like-kind recast stacking rounds and topping the pool up CAPPED at the original startingShield (which never itself rises), and the pool sitting on the one damage door every source already comes through. REVIEW FIXES pinned: a BUSTED shield resigns as incumbent so a recast becomes its own live pool and the corpse is never revived (the un-resigned entry used to swallow every later cast and re-extend its own rounds, leaving Shield permanently dead after one bust - found independently by two reviewers); SetHealth(0) bypasses the pool so drowning and the lethal exhaustion collapse still kill, with all four callers pinned to the bypass; and the dungeon drown call restored to passing its ENTITY (it had lost the argument entirely, so drowning in a dungeon never dealt a point - a bug pre-dating X1). X2 PARITY (the three gaps the X1 review left open): absorption recomputing its chance from the TARGET level at absorb time while resistance stays frozen at the CASTER level (DFU's one asymmetry - TryEffectBasedAbsorption reads entity.Level, TryResistance goes through RollChance/ChanceValue), both reading ONE incumbent rather than a sum (a 50%% item beside a 50%% spell used to read as a certainty) while Elemental Resistance keeps its genuine additive sum, and all three riding AssignBundle's no-magnitude saving throw so a non-CasterOnly defence buff can be refused WHOLE while a self-cast never is. FOUR DFU QUIRKS PRESERVED as real behaviour rather than smoothed: a mid-stream palette desyncs the stream (streamPosition += 768 is clobbered by the tail assignment), frameDelay is recomputed from audioBuffer for EVERY block so a video block inherits the last audio delay and one before any audio block throws, a swallowed exception does NOT advance the stream so a bad block parks the reader forever, and Audio_StartFrame consumes its data before rejecting a bad PlaybackRate. The null-block 'not counted' quirk is pinned on real data (currentBlock == blocks - nulls, with 83 nulls in DAG2). C#'s IndexOutOfRangeException on Color32[] is emulated with an explicit bounds throw, because a JS typed array drops out-of-range writes SILENTLY and would have completed the block. Plus the player: the frame gate, the audio clip built verbatim (srcLength+2, pos=1, (b-128)/128) scheduled on the WebAudio clock, and the release-then-upload that stops a texture leaking per frame + THE SPLASH LETTERBOX, caught by the EYEBALL and not by a pin: the first live shot of ANIM0001 had a sky-blue border, the renderer's Iliac Bay clear showing around the native panel - the THIRD time that has bitten (U21 blacked it for the menu, U21b for chargen). Pinned synthetically so it runs without ARENA2: the FIRST quad of the frame loop is the solid backdrop, covering the whole real canvas rather than the 320x200 panel, in the SHARED MENU_BACKDROP constant rather than a third hand-written black. Mutation-proven both ways - dropping the backdrop, and sizing it to the native panel + AUDIT 19: the ERROR BOUNDARY playVideo never had - the reader throws OUTSIDE ReadBlock's try by design, so a truncated video killed the rAF chain and left the promise permanently pending, which main.js awaits, so it was a permanent black screen and the file's own NEVER TRAPS header was false; pinned on both the reader half and the draw half. THE SPLASH WAS UNCONDITIONALLY SILENT and the source blamed the wrong thing: the player resolves its AudioContext once at construction and nothing had booted an engine by splash time, so there was no context to resolve - the gesture rule gates whether a context RUNS, not whether one EXISTS. The context is now built eagerly and suspended, and is no longer gated on DAGGER.SND loading, which music and video do not read. Measured live: 70 audio clips scheduled where it scheduled zero. Plus the frame pin that compared the buffer WITH ITSELF - it reached through .buffer, discarding offset and length, so uploading a ONE-PIXEL view passed; the renderer had the same blind spot, which is why neither caught it + THE SUSPENDED-CLOCK PIN: a context that exists but is not running (autoplay policy, repeat visit) has currentTime pinned at 0, and pacing on it froze the splash at its first frame - solid black for ANIM0001; a non-running context is treated as absent (wall clock, silent, no clips), a stub with no state field still counts as running, and the boot-order pin now demands ensureAudio UN-awaited with the context minted in its synchronous prefix, so the splash no longer parks on black behind the DAGGER.SND + MIDI.BSA loads |
| music.test.js | 55 | A5 MUSIC, and the pins are split by what can be PROVEN. The PORT half is SongManager.cs: the playlists whole rather than sampled (a sampled table lets a drifted row through - the 17d lesson), DFU's DUPLICATE entries preserved because a duplicate doubles that song's odds (FM_DNGN1 and 15FM twice in the FM dungeon list, FSNEAK2 twice in FM sneaking), and SelectCurrentSong arm by arm - taverns index gameDays DIRECTLY so they walk in sequence day to day, dungeons seed DFRandom with unknown2 XOR (region<<8) with the region masked to a byte as DFU's cast does, everything else seeds on gameDays so a song is stable all day, and A LIST OF ONE returns index 0 WITHOUT consuming the generator (the length>1 guard - if it seeded, entering a temple would shift every later roll in the session). Corpus-gated: every name in every playlist resolves to a real MIDI.BSA record and the picks DECODE, so a typo cannot become a song that silently never plays. The SCHEDULER half is pure: the lookahead window is HALF-OPEN (at 480 PPQN a closed one retriggers every beat), the window provably exceeds the wake interval or notes fall through the gap, and channel state FOLDS FORWARD so tick-0 program/volume survives into second three instead of the opening playing on the wrong instrument and silently 'fixing itself'. The SYNTH half is OURS and is pinned for STRUCTURE, not taste - there is no source to be right against, and a pin asserting 'program 48 is a sawtooth' would assert my own choices back at me (the vacuous-pin shape two audits have caught here). So: all 128 GM programs resolve into their GM family with finite ordered envelopes and a non-zero attack (a zero ramp clicks), out-of-range programs and notes CLAMP rather than throw because the reader hands through whatever the bytes said, no percussion key is ever silent, and no conversion can put a NaN into the audio graph. A440 at note 69 is the one piece of real law and is exact. Mutation-proven eight ways + A5b FM AND THE OUTDOOR/TAVERN ARMS: the voice bank is now two-operator FM - the OPL synthesis Daggerfall was scored for, which is why the archive carries an F*/FM* arrangement of nearly every song - and its ratios are OURS, pinned for structure like the subtractive specs were (no OPL bank exists to read: it lived in HMI's driver, ARENA2 has no .AD/.BNK/.OPL and the song headers carry a device map and zeros where patches would be, checked byte by byte). Every program resolves to a finite voice with a POSITIVE carrier ratio (0 Hz is silence) and a NON-ZERO index decay (exponentialRamp needs it). AssignPlaylist's outdoor arms verbatim - night overrides weather entirely, Fog folds in with Overcast and Thunder with Rain, and an unrecognised weather falls to Sunny by DFU's OWN default arm rather than going silent. THE CORPUS PIN EARNED ITS KEEP: it caught a NIGHT_SONGS_FM list I had extrapolated from the GM shape - DFU's FM night array has SIX entries to the GM list's seven and there is no 10FM record in MIDI.BSA at all, which is exactly the asymmetry DFU's own :818 note describes. Plus the host sweep - all four hosts wired, taverns taking the DIRECT gameDays arm + AUDIT 19: the SIXTEEN playlists the first pass never ported and the fourteen AssignPlaylist arms that went with them - castle, court, shop, Mages Guild, plain interior, fighter trainers, palace and the FM tavern, every one resolving to a real MIDI.BSA record, so 'not ported' meant music the player could never hear rather than a missing asset. The whole switch is pinned arm by arm, including the two that LOOK like the day/night gate and are not - a dungeon exterior and a graveyard take NIGHT songs at any hour, pinned at noon - and the arrested check that precedes the switch entirely. The building fold too: eleven mercantile types collapsing to one Shop, a GuildHall that is the Mages Guild only when its faction says so, a Temple that is FighterTrainers for the Fighters Guild and otherwise takes its god's alignment, and DFU's own `default` so an unmapped building plays the interior list rather than nothing. Mutation-proven four ways - reading the clock for a graveyard, moving the arrested check, making every GuildHall the Mages Guild, dropping one type from the shop fold + AUDIT 19 THE 1:1 PASS - SongManager's ENGINE, not just its tables. The port had the playlists and none of the loop: hosts called playFrom at moments they chose, where DFU rebuilds a CONTEXT every frame and reacts to the difference (UpdateSong :198-231). Three behaviours are unreachable without it and are now pinned - a new DAY or a new LOCATION re-picks even when the playlist is IDENTICAL (a playlist-only comparison cannot express it, the lists being the same object), locationIndex is in the context at all, and a finished song re-evaluates the context before the next is chosen. PlayCurrentSong's guard is pinned BOTH ways: it must not restart the song already playing, and it MUST replay one that ended - DFU's guard reads Song == currentSong AND IsPlaying, and the second half is what makes a finished song eligible again. Plus UpdatePlayerMusicEnvironment's whole switch with HomePoor sitting among the DUNGEON exteriors, the rect test that precedes it, the castle arm, the temple/god faction tables index-for-index, PlayNext/PlayPrevious wrapping at both ends, and StopPlaying latching. THE TWO INPUTS EARLIER PASSES CALLED UNAVAILABLE ARE BOTH REAL, pinned on the archive: the dungeon seed (unknown2 XOR region) over 4,232 dungeons giving 3,769 distinct keys and a near-uniform spread across the 15-song list, and the building faction id over 634 temples resolving to all three alignments plus the Fighters Guild, with 119 of 237 guild halls the Mages Guild + CONTINUOUS CONTROLS: pitch bend and CC7 volume used to be read once at NOTE START, so the 15,017 controller events the archive puts INSIDE a sounding note were all inaudible until the next note began. Bends are scheduled on the sounding voices' detune params at their own tick, and channel volume moved to a PER-CHANNEL gain node - which also stops it fighting the per-note envelope, as modulating the voice's own gain would have. And the ARRESTED flag, whose single consumer is the music: PlayerEntity.CourtWindow sets it and OnPop clears it, and AssignPlaylist checks it FIRST, so without it CourtSongs was unreachable + AUDIT 21 pinned what a mutation campaign found LOOSE in the 1:1 engine: the two guild faction ids (FactionFile.cs:89-90 - a wrong id silently routes the Mages Guild and the fighter trainers to the plain interior), DFRegion's LOCATION_TYPES enum WHOLE (the environment switch is written by NAME, so a drifted value moves a location into another arm and nothing else notices), the manager actually ROUTING the tavern arm (pinned by walking the list in sequence twice around, which no other arm does), playPrevious/playNext at the boundary rather than near it (testing only from index 0 left the comparison free to be `< 1`), and playSong initialising TRUE + AUDIT 21 F1: the director was fed AFTER the modal early return in both outdoor hosts. worldModes.frame() returns TRUE for interior and dungeon and musicContext() returns null ONLY for exterior - the SAME predicate - so the context was fed exclusively on frames where the overlay was guaranteed null, and the whole interior/dungeon music path was dead code. A source ORDER pin, stated as the weak instrument it is; the real proof is tools/musicHostProbe.mjs, which walks a browser into a real building and reads the resolved environment on both sides (outdoors GDAY___D.HMI/city -> inside 23.HMI/interior, and with the ordering reverted the inside reads city and the song never changes) + SONGFILES.CS AND ENUMTOFILENAME (2026-08-25, with the PlaySong quest action): the enum is the SET Enum.IsDefined answers for - 133 members, song_none included, the gaps (song_dungeon4, song_14) undefined, and the compare ORDINAL so a shouted member name is undefined in C# too; EnumToFilename is strip-song_/upper-case/.HMI and is INJECTIVE; and the two spellings are ONE set - every entry of every songManager playlist is reachable from some enum member, which catches a typo on either side without the archive present + THE AUDIO AUDIT (2026-08-25), run because the music "just seemed off" and it was, in three ways. THE DRUM MAP STOPPED AT NOTE 51 and percussionSpec resolves by NEAREST NEIGHBOUR, so every key above it answered as the ride cymbal. Measured over the real archive that was 25 of the 30 drum notes in use and 14,000+ hits - Daggerfall's percussion is hand drums and bells rather than a rock kit (tambourine 3,364, jingle bell 2,561, congas 3,874, claves 919, agogo 650, every one above 51), so the ENTIRE rhythm section played one wash of cymbal. The map runs 35..87 now, each key owning ITSELF, with the nearest-neighbour fallback kept for keys outside it because a silent key is worse than an approximate one. A CORPUS PIN keeps the measurement that found it: the archive's own notes must land on as many distinct sounds as there are notes. It groups by spec MINUS the `key` field, and says why - `key` carries the note identity, so a first measurement that left it in reported 30 sounds where there were 5. `drop` is new and is not just a longer list: a falling pitch is what makes a drum read as a drum, and holding pitch is what makes a triangle, cowbell, claves or agogo read as itself. ARTICULATION BEATS FAMILY: fmSpec resolves with `program >> 3`, a fair base, but it put harp and timpani in `strings` and they came out BOWED - one is plucked and rings, the other is struck and thuds. Eight programs the archive actually plays now override their family, and the pin is structural (a plucked thing must not have a bowed envelope) rather than taste, as this module's header requires. THE SUSTAIN PEDAL was dropped entirely - 593 CC64 events - and it is the ONLY ignored controller that changes what you hear: CC123/CC121 matter to a device that must be told to stop, and this scheduler cannot hang a note because an HMI note-on carries its own duration. Computed from the WHOLE event list rather than a live flag, because a note scheduled now may need to ring past the lookahead window. TWO MUTANTS PROVED THE IMPLEMENTATION HAD DEAD CODE: `Math.max(durationTicks, up - startTick)` can never pick the first argument, because the guard `end < up` already means the second is larger - so the max was removed rather than left looking like a safeguard, and the never-shortens pin was re-aimed at the case that actually reaches the decision. A third mutant showed every fixture was made of CC64 events and so none of them could tell whether the controller number was checked at all. CLEARED WITH EVIDENCE, so nobody re-chases them: zero tempo meta events in 131 songs (the constant secondsPerTick is right), CC105's 2,572 events are exactly 1,286 ones and 1,286 zeros against an archive of 1,286 tracks so it is a per-track marker and not music, no RPN 0,0 anywhere so the hardcoded 2-semitone bend range is correct, and pan does reach a real StereoPanner. 17 mutations, 15 dead, 2 recorded equivalent (both at the pedal boundary, where `up - startTick` and `durationTicks` are provably the same number) |
| hmi.test.js | 9 | MIDI.BSA, the ELEVENTH format reader and the first with NO DFU SOURCE AT ALL - DFU never reads this archive (DaggerfallSongPlayer loads 133 pre-converted .mid files plus an SF2 from Unity Resources), so every claim is established from the shipped bytes and corpus-gated over the whole retail archive. The slice was commissioned as XMI and the bytes refused: there is no FORM/XDIR/CAT/ XMID chunk anywhere, only 131 "HMI-MIDISONG061595" songs holding 1286 "HMI-MIDITRACK" tracks, and HMI's deltas are ordinary MIDI VLQs where XMI's are interval counts. TWO INVARIANTS, and AUDIT 19 corrected how strong the first one is. The byte-exact gate - all 1286 tracks consuming to FF 2F 00 exactly at the next track's offset - was described as proving the 0xFE event sizes, and it does not: 0x14 = 1 or 2 lands every track byte-exactly too, while blowing D1.HMI from 26,926 to 2,123,950 ticks. It DOES determine 0x15 = 7 (size 6 makes 130 of 131 songs throw) and the self-checking 0x10. What discriminates the rest is a SECOND invariant now pinned: the tracks of a song END TOGETHER - 123 of 131 songs have every track finishing on the same tick with the decoded sizes, and 4 of 131 with a wrong 0x14. 0x12/0x13 appear once each in the whole archive and are determined by NEITHER check; they are recorded as an assumption rather than dressed up as proven. The reader also ENFORCES the gate now instead of computing it and discarding it, and an unknown status byte throws rather than decoding as a pitch bend on a phantom channel. The track table (u32@0xE4 count, u32@0xE8 offsets) is pinned against the scanned signatures at 0 mismatches. Plus the merged tick-ordered event stream with note-offs sorting before note-ons at equal ticks EXCEPT a zero-duration note's own; and the corpus quirks preserved rather than smoothed - SUNNYDAY track 5's real 1,192,560-tick delta, TAVERN track 22 the only track whose header count disagrees with its stream and the only holder of FE 12/13, FOLK3's two FE 14 and no FE 15. Nothing decodes to garbage silently: unknown FE subtypes, misaligned data bytes, over-long VLQs and missing end-of-track all THROW with song name and offset |

Its headline was not a wrong constant: createMusicDirector - the single seam through which every host feeds SongManager - had ZERO behavioural coverage, and three mutations that each silence a large part of the game left the WHOLE SUITE green. Reversing the spread so `base` wins kills all interior and dungeon music (the AUDIT 21 F1 defect re-introduced by a different edit than the one F1's pin watches, since that pin checks statement ORDER in the host files); `songEnded: false` kills DFU's whole !IsPlaying arm; `play: () => {}` means no music plays anywhere. The sinks are injectable now - nothing in the hosts passes them - so this file drives the real director, and one pin patches the module singleton to prove the DEFAULT sink is not a no-op, which the injected harness cannot see. F3 CASTLE MUSIC was unreachable: both call sites hardcoded insideDungeonCastle false behind a flag claiming "no castle-block detection yet", while rdbLayout had computed castleBlock verbatim on every block all along and five real castle blocks were already pinned - and the same hardcode left ambientEffects' doNotPlayInCastle READ by the code and WRITTEN by nobody. F4 an unresolved temple forced Interior where SongManager.cs:494-518 has NO else and the environment HOLDS - the flag blamed a missing temple-faction table that was in the same file, hiding the real divergence behind a retired excuse; two existing pins encoded the old behaviour and are corrected in place with the reason recorded. F11 the clock started at 0 where DaggerfallDateTime's classicGameStartTime is 523530 - DAY 363 - and gameDays is both the DFRandom seed for every non-dungeon playlist and the tavern index, so every song on a given in-game date differed from DFU's. F5, F6 and F7 are pin-quality: locationChanged, the unseeded Mages Guild arm and AUDIT 19 F5's own `>>> 0` fix were each one edit from silent loss. The last test is a structural RULE for the host half, written as a field-set comparison rather than a substring match, because a rule that greps for "factionId" passes on `factionId: 0` - which is the mutation that has to be caught. 15 mutations, 15 killed + M-FM 2026-08-25: AUDIO/ALTERNATEMUSIC REACHED NOTHING. The whole feature was built and nothing joined it - every FM playlist ported since A5 with its DFU quirks intact, outdoorPlaylist and playlistFor branching on `fm` all along, SongManager taking it, createMusicDirector accepting and forwarding it - while all three hosts called createMusicDirector() with NO ARGUMENTS, so the checkbox the settings screen offers moved nothing at all. The read goes in the FACTORY, not the call sites: three call sites are three chances to forget and one read is none, which is the lesson ensureAudio's own header already records. `null` asks the setting and an explicit true/false overrides it, so this file's existing fm-driven pins stay independent of the setting's state. Driven END TO END rather than by grep - the setting is flipped and the SONG THAT PLAYS is asserted to come from the FM set or the General MIDI set, which needs the two sets to differ and asserts that first. The fixture needed a second draft: the director DERIVES its environment through musicEnvironment rather than taking one, so passing `environment: 'dungeonInterior'` straight in got the outdoor day song and proved nothing. DFU swaps the nineteen playlist fields ONCE in SongManager.Start, so a mid-session toggle does not move the music until the scene reboots - read at construction here for the same reason and with the same consequence, verbatim rather than an oversight. 4 mutations, 4 dead |
| audit21_hosts.test.js | 10 | AUDIT 21's HOSTS lane. The six scene hosts have ZERO node execution coverage, which is this project's most repeated bug shape - a feature lands in one host and not its siblings and every test stays green - so these are RULES over the host sources plus behavioural pins on the shared machinery underneath. F3 LEVELLING OUTSIDE A DUNGEON SILENTLY AUTO-SPENT YOUR ATTRIBUTE POINTS: all three above-ground carriers built a player ticker with no onLevelUp, so advancement.js took its headless arm and dumped every point into your LOWEST stats with no message and no choice - the same XP built a different character depending on where you were standing. Fixing it needed a second half nobody had noticed: townTalk understood Escape and Enter/E and worldModes passed the raw key CODE, so even once the screen opened it could not be driven (a LevelUpScreen wants up/down and plus/minus). Both route through overlayAction now, the same map routeKey feeds the dungeon host. The behavioural pin's FIRST draft was vacuous - its fixture never levelled at all, so both arms trivially matched - which is the exact shape this audit kept finding elsewhere; it now asserts a real level-up fires and that the headless arm visibly spends where the hooked arm leaves the stats alone. F4 the 3D audio LISTENER was moved by world, exterior and dungeonContext and by nothing in worldModes, so in INTERIOR mode it stayed parked at the town's world position while interiorContext played door sounds at interior-LOCAL coordinates against a finite maxDistance - out of range entirely; the rule also pins that the call sits BEFORE the mode branch, since moving it inside the dungeon arm restores the defect for interiors. 9 mutations, 9 killed. NOT filed, and recorded in Ledger B instead: the outdoor rain loop following you indoors is DFU (WeatherManager.Update returns early when inside, so Presets stays Rain); F6 DYING OUTSIDE A DUNGEON DID NOTHING AT ALL - a guard beat you to 0 HP in the street and the game carried on, you kept walking and the guards kept hitting a corpse; four paths wrote player health and exactly ONE (the dungeon's) checked, so the check moved to the write. One damage door in playerEntity.js, a presenter registered by whichever host is live (the same shape F2 gave the world clock), and the door fires on the TRANSITION rather than the state - firing whenever health reads zero re-presented the screen once per effect round, which the dungeon had been hiding behind an instanceof guard in its presenter. F8 swinging a weapon INSIDE A BUILDING cost no fatigue and trained no skill: WeaponManager drains the swing whatever it hits, the dungeon and the city guards both do it, and the interior rig did neither - its bow arm tallied one hand-picked skill and drained nothing, disagreeing with the dungeon's own bow arm, which takes tallySwingSkills because a bow always reaches DFU's tally arm. 14 more mutations, 14 killed. Two of the survivors in the first pass were artefacts of the MUTATION HARNESS, not weak pins - it replaced only the first occurrence, so removing one of three setDeathPresenter calls looked survivable; it replaces all occurrences now; F7 NO HUD ABOVE GROUND - ?world and ?exterior drew no status bar at all (no health, no fatigue, no magicka, no compass) while guards, falls, per-minute fatigue drain and disease attribute drain all applied, and inside a BUILDING it vanished too because drawHud lived inside dungeonContext.drawFoes which the interior arm never calls. ui/hud.js was already host-agnostic, so this is the art loaded once per host and drawn last. The rule's other half is tools/hudProbe.mjs, which boots both exterior hosts for real and counts saturated pixels where the bars belong - calibrated BY MUTATION (1600/1799 with the draw, 438 without, floor 1000), because the grass and plaster behind the bars are themselves colourful and a bare 'is there any colour' test does not discriminate. Two earlier drafts of that probe are recorded in it as warnings: one called readPixels on a context re-got from the canvas, which reads a swapped buffer and reported 0% while the screenshot plainly showed the bars; the other compared PNG byte sizes, which cannot tell a textured sky from a status bar. The F7 mutation also exposed a flaw in every source rule in this file - they grepped RAW text, so removing the drawHud CALL left the word in the comment above it and the rule passed; all of them read comment-stripped source now. 28 mutations across the four hosts campaigns, 28 killed |

WAVE 27 then CORRECTED THIS WAVE'S OWN SHIPPED BUG, caught by the scout that landed after it: locationCompassDirection indexed `mapTable[i]` by the loop counter and the pin called that last-match-wins, where C# reads `MapTable[MapNameLookup[locations[i]]]` (TalkManager.cs:1263-1266) and MapNameLookup is built FIRST-WINS (MapsFile.cs:1082-1083) - so duplicates resolve to the FIRST row and the loop's repeats are wasted work, not a rule. Two names differing only in CASE still take the last, because the ToLower compare matches both while the dictionary keys stay exact-case; a name the lookup does not hold is skipped rather than defaulted to row 0; and a region record with no lookup at all answers never-mind rather than throwing, which dropping the guard survived every other pin here |

| audit24_wave32.test.js | 6 | AUDIT 24 wave 32, one broker and many subscribers. DFU has ONE EntityEffectBroker with a single lastGameMinute raising OnNewMagicRound, and ONE EntityEffectManager PER ENTITY, all subscribed; the port had the broker and exactly one subscriber plus an ad-hoc loop in the dungeon host. So above ground a foe's Continuous Damage never took a round, its poison never fired, a drained-to-zero attribute never killed it, and a paralysed encounter foe stayed paralysed for the session - the round that would have expired the paralysis was the round that never ran. runMagicRounds splits into claimMagicRounds (the marker, the cap, the half-open window - consumed once per frame) and runMagicRoundsFor (one subscriber's DoMagicRound over a claimed window, marker-free, so N subscribers all get the same rounds); createPlayerTicker grew subscribe() and each host registers its pools through the same hoisted foeSinks the cast engine takes. PARALYSIS: both exterior pools passed the literal `false` for EnemyMotor's paralyzed argument and ran the attack machine unconditionally (EnemyMotor.HandleParalysis :247-260 drops CanAct; EnemyAttack returns at the top of Update :91-94 and FixedUpdate :55-57) - and because those pools resolve damage off the MOBILE's frames rather than by suppressing the mobile update the way the dungeon does, the hit and shoot frames need their own gate or a swing already in flight lands. The dungeon's own foe loop ran [floor(clock at frame start), floor(clock now)) off its own arithmetic - no catch-up, no 2880 cap - and now rides the claimed window. And PlayerEntity.Update's per-minute loop (:452-459, the 112-day reputation normalisation) is NOT the broker's: its own lastGameMinutes, no cap, and the minute VALUES [last, now) with no +1 - AUDIT 23 had written it inside the broker's round loop and inherited the `r + 1`, so the port normalised one game minute late and would have missed any boundary more than 2880 minutes back. Two mutants are recorded as EQUIVALENT rather than dressed up as kills: a subscriber that also advances the marker (the load re-anchor corrects it before it can skip a round) and the Number.isFinite guard on the entity marker |

EnemyMotor.HandleParalysis (:247-260) sets mobile.FreezeAnims = true inside `if (IsParalyzed)` and false on the line AFTER the closing brace, with two plain field assignments in between, no reader, and no third writer in the whole tree - so a paralysed enemy's animation is never frozen in DFU: UpdateToIdleOrMoveAnim runs after the `if (CanAct)` gate and puts a stationary one into Idle, and UpdateOrientation has no FreezeAnims check at all, so the sprite keeps turning to face the player. The port's dungeon host skipped the whole mobile update and redrew a cached output - freezing the FACING as well as the frame - and quoted the cancelled comment while doing it, in the source and twice in Combat.md. What stops the blow is EnemyAttack's early return (:91-94). AND THE LATCH: DoMeleeDamage/ShootArrow are set by AnimateEnemy at the -1 marker and the ONLY writer of false in the tree is EnemyAttack.Update:100, which the paralysis return at :93 precedes - so a swing that reaches its damage frame while paralysed is not dropped, it waits and lands on the first unparalysed frame. The port's flags were per-frame edges cleared at the top of every update; they are latches now, named after the DFU members, and all three pools consume-and-clear. Pinned behaviourally (the flag survives twenty further updates and only the consumer's clear sticks), structurally (every latch read in a host has a matching clear, counted both ways), and - because the whole wave rests on one claim about DFU - against the vendored C# itself: the reset outside the guard, exactly two writers, and no IsParalyzed anywhere in the animation layer |

ObstacleCheck (:1140-1201, a capsule of half the controller radius swept radius/sqrt(2) from the climbable-step height, cleared by an action door or by a taller re-cast aimed a unit ahead and a unit up), FallCheck (:1204-1218, a downward ray from a unit ahead reaching height/2 + 1.5 - which starts at the CENTRE, so the real limit is 1.5 below the feet, pinned both sides of that edge) and FindDetour (:1002-1137, the flyer/swimmer vertical dodge, the two-second unstuck reset, the five-second committed hand chosen at random then by signed angle then flipped, and the 45-degree sweep bounded at eight tries - whose EIGHTH probe is a full 360, so a boxed-in foe walks straight back into what blocked it) all live in EnemyAI, so all three pools inherit them. The two exemptions the port's collider cannot express (the obstacle is the combat target; the obstacle is a loot pile) are documented rather than written as dead branches - entities are not in the collider. The door arm is real: doors are their own buckets and the AI asks the host through an `isActionDoor` dep, the same registry OpenDoors reads. Also pins `collider.capsuleCast` - a ray bundle honouring Unity's contract, including the leading cap (a real cast touches `radius` before the axis arrives and reports the distance TRAVELLED; the first cut reported the raw ray distance and foes stopped 0.1 short of seeing the wall) - and the DF_WALK_BASE cycle that a module-level `CAPSULE_RADIUS / Math.SQRT2` turned into a ReferenceError across twelve files |

Every `world.x` the quest system calls must be MOUNTED on the host's questWorld or named in the test's PENDING table with the reason it cannot be answered yet - and a PENDING row for a seam that IS mounted (a stale excuse) or that nothing calls any more (dead weight) fails too. The optional-chaining seam shape is the port's charter and also a trapdoor: a seam nobody mounts is a ported law that evaporates in silence with a green suite, and it had happened four times over - `discoverLocation` (the corpus's 193 `reveal` lines discovered nothing), `addNote` (the readmap journal note), `currentRegionRace` (every unmapped Person race fell to -1 instead of the region's) and `getRandomText` (%oth had no TEXT.RSC to read) were all declared in Q3 and wired to nothing. Plus the questorData STRUCT: StaticNPC.NPCData is a C# struct with no null, and an un-setup questor holds all zeros - the port used null and THREE reads dereference it unguarded (topicTree's GetPersonBuildingKey and its quest-topic rebuild, sceneMount's questor billboard pick), every one reachable through `add <sym> as questor`, the route to isQuestor that never calls SetupQuestorNPC. And wave 10: ParseQuest's swallow-all catch (the port had none, so one broken QBN threw out of the guild picker instead of answering null - and loadQuest's `if (!quest)` arm sat unreachable), TeleportPc's SiteLink write moved back above the transport guard and its marker-array read unguarded as C# has it, and a late destroy event no longer nulls a RECOUPLED resource link (C# writes to a shadowing PARAMETER there, so its field is never cleared at all - Unity's fake-null is what makes the clear look right). Plus wave 11's gate: no action may carry a createNew-only field that a RESTORE leaves undefined - a restore mints through the (Quest) constructor and walks saveShape, so a field set only in createNew comes back UNDEFINED where C# gets 0 from its field default. PlaySound was the one: `this.timesPlayed++` ran on undefined, NaN failed `NaN <= count`, and the sound never played again for the rest of the save. The gate now covers the BRIDGE's ctx surface too, which has the same trapdoor: RemoveNpcQuestor - the offer window's one constructor side effect - had been carried by npcSession since TK-iv and called by nothing, so an offered townsperson never left npcsWithWork and was re-offered the same quest for ever; MakePcDiseased and CurePcDisease were likewise declared and unmounted over an S18 system ported in its own slice. And THE NEW-GAME START: StartGameBehaviour.cs:444-456 starts THREE things on a new character - `_TUTOR__`, `_BRISIEN` and then the InitAtGameStart list - and the port called only the third, which with vanilla tables is EMPTY. A new character started no quests at all and the MAIN QUEST never began; both files have been vendored the whole time and neither had ever been parsed |

| crashreport.test.js | 11 | THE CRASH REPORT and the never-trap under it (2026-08-21, from a real player's screenshot). The overlay printed `err.stack` ALONE, which is a browser-dependent decision it did not know it was making: V8 folds "Name: message" into `stack` and SpiderMonkey does NOT, so every crash report from a Firefox player arrived with the one line that says WHAT was null missing - and one did, two frames and no message. crashText composes the head itself and is pinned over both stack dialects, a Chrome stack not printed twice, the non-Error shapes a rejection can carry (a bare string is NOT dressed up as "Error: ..."), an error with no stack still keeping window.onerror's coordinates, and main.js registering exactly ONE listener per event (it had TWO for unhandledrejection, the second able only to overwrite the first with a worse format). Then the crash the report could not name: drawMesh dereferenced `mesh.vao` and iterated `mesh.subMeshes` with no guard, so ONE model missing from the player's own ARCH3D threw out of the frame loop and killed the whole scene. The real method is driven against a recording `this` - null, undefined, {}, a vao with no subMeshes and an EMPTY subMeshes list all return having touched no GL state, the warning fires once and not sixty times a second, and a GOOD mesh still draws (count x3, byte offset x4). Plus the producer: interiorContext pushed `await getGpuMesh(id)` into its draw list unguarded - getGpuMesh returns null for an id this data set lacks and CACHES it, and cpuModels is written only on the success path, so the same placement read `cpu.positions` off undefined one line later. It was the only builder in the port that did not skip the placement; the sibling guards are pinned too, so this fails if one of THEM loses it. THE HUNT THEN FOUND THE ACTUAL PRODUCER, and it was a null MATRIX, not a null mesh: ensureArrowModel pushed `{gpu, object:{matrix:null}}` and left the matrix for the next updateMissiles pass, but the push lands in a MICROTASK (it is async and its one caller does not await it) and both hosts draw dynamicDraws BEFORE they call drawFoes - so the next frame drew a null matrix and `uniformMatrix4fv` threw, because Float32List is a non-nullable WebIDL union. Firing a bow killed the frame loop. Worse, an arrow retired point-blank while the load was pending left an ORPHAN whose matrix nothing would ever fill, throwing on every frame after. The matrix is built at push time now, the dead check matches arrowFlight's, and the host ORDERING both depend on is pinned so a reshuffle re-reads the test rather than resurrecting the bug. The interior half was corrected too: the build died THIRTY-SEVEN LINES before that placement guard could run - collect() stores an undefined cpu model, layoutInterior dereferences it on both arms, and the climate pass reads `cpu.subMeshes` off the same undefined (`?? []` guards the value, never the receiver) - so pressing E on that door did nothing at all, for ever, with a raw TypeError instead of the doctrine's warn. Same three traps in the dungeon's action-door arm, which lacked the `if (!gpu) continue` its own placement loop fifty lines above has. Seven mutations, 7 killed |

DFU runs this as a coroutine on `WaitForSeconds(1f/speed)`, a FIXED step: the port pins that a 30Hz host and a 144Hz host show the same frame at the same wall time and that a partial tick advances nothing (the first draft of that pin sat exactly on 1/fps and caught its own float rounding - the boundary is a coin-toss, not a behaviour). Last is THE FOUR HOSTS SWEEP, applied before the fact for once: the static-flat batch is built at exactly four sites and drawn from five (worldModes draws both contexts), and the pin fails if a site builds without arming or a host draws without ticking. Both mount sites are the same six lines in two files - exactly how one gets fixed and the other does not - so the pin covers arming, dropping the clock on retire, and the ORPHAN window the arrow crash taught: the mount is async, so a missile that retires while its texture warms must not publish a batch afterwards, or it draws at its fire position for the rest of the scene. hostMagic is shared by three hosts, so its clock rides its own update rather than each host's frame. Six more mutations, 6 killed. SLICE 3 unpicked the KEY SCHEME that had blocked the rest: the dropped-loot piles and both NPC-corpse modules hand-wrote `record#0` INTO the record they passed, which is the same texture key by a different route and the exact reason they could not be armed at slice 2 - appending a frame index to a record already ending in one reads `20#0#2`. The record is bare and the frame is a field everywhere now, so the draw builds the key one way, and the pin covers the half a refactor like this forgets: the floating-origin recenter DESTROYS and recreates corpse batches, so a rebuilt batch needs its frame carried too. It carries the two things every person in the world was missing: a flat's CAPTION - which the quest macro =symbol_ resolves through, a seam the quest machine declared at Q2 with NO production provider, so every =person_ expanded to nothing - and its FACE INDEX into TFAC00I0.RCI, the portrait the talk window's empty 64x64 frame wants. Two quirks are kept as written and both are pinned: a DUPLICATE archive/record keeps the FIRST block, because DFU guards its insert with ContainsKey where a plain assignment would let the last one win; and a line of SPACES is NOT a block separator, because IsNullOrEmpty runs on the RAW line and the Trim happens after it - so whitespace joins the block as an empty string and shifts every field below it, and a "tidier" reader would parse a different file than classic does. Plus the filename guard being a silent no-op rather than an error, CRLF, the ChildGard "?" gender mark read THROUGH rather than dropping the flat, and int.Parse THROWING on a non-number where Number() would answer NaN and travel silently as a face index.
