| staticnpc.test.js | 8 | NPC1 - StaticNPC.cs's identity layer, the data under every person standing in a shop, temple, guild hall or palace. Classic stores no NPC table: a person IS their position and their faction, and their name, gender, race and portrait all fall out of those two through a seeded PRNG - which is why the same shopkeeper is the same person every time you walk in and why they differ between one building and the next. THREE PINS EARN THEIR KEEP. The NAME SEED is `position ^ buildingKey + locationIndex`, and C# binds + tighter than ^, so it groups as position ^ (buildingKey + locationIndex) - the natural left-to-right reading renames every NPC in the game, and the pin's inputs are chosen so the two groupings DISAGREE (the first draft used a triple where they coincide and would have passed against the wrong one). FACTION.TXT's race column is a DIFFERENT enum from Races, not an offset of it - FactionRaces is Nord 0, Khajiit 1, Redguard 2, Breton 3... where Races is Breton 1, Redguard 2, Nord 3... so the orders disagree as well as the bases, and "the same list one lower" is wrong for every entry but one; the port's first draft computed exactly that and every NPC would have taken the wrong race with the suite green. And the position hash is pinned to LITERAL values, because re-deriving the formula inside the assertion pins nothing - it passes against any implementation that matches itself. Plus gender being flag bit 32 and nothing else, an INDIVIDUAL faction answering its own name where everyone else is seeded and STABLE, an unfactioned person taking the race of the region (which is what makes a village look like its province), and the four FACTION.TXT races with no Races counterpart falling through to that same fallback |
| hostdeps.test.js | 4 | THE DEPS A HOST HANDS OVER MUST COVER WHAT THE CONTEXT UNPACKS. Reported from play as `d is not a function` with a minified stack, and the chain had THREE links with only the middle one ever checked by anything: the two exterior hosts build a `pipeline: {...}` bag for worldModes, worldModes destructures that and hands a bag on, and dungeonContext destructures THAT and calls what it got. uploadRecordFrame was missing from the first link - it arrived undefined, travelled two hops without a word, and threw on the first enemy frame in a dungeon entered from the world, while the standalone ?dungeon scene (which spreads the whole pipeline) was fine. Nothing failed at build time, because a missing PROPERTY is not a missing export and rollup had nothing to say. These four read BOTH ends of each seam and assert the caller covers the callee: what worldModes unpacks from pipeline against what each host supplies, what buildDungeonContext and buildInteriorContext unpack against what worldModes hands them, the standalone scene still spreading the pipeline whole, and what the hosts unpack from createDataPipeline against what it actually returns. Proven to FAIL on the real bug and pass on the fix |
| ledger.test.js | 4 | L2 - the PORT-LEDGER's derivable figures, gated. The 2026-08-24 re-sweep found twenty stale rows in section C and FOUR were wrong in their NUMBERS rather than their substance: "seventeen unbuilt guild service windows" when the answer was two, "thirty unported effects" when it was six, "the only trace of banking is a weight constant" against a 419-line module. Prose cannot notice that, and it is the drift that most misleads - someone reading "seventeen windows to build" plans a different week than someone reading "two". So every figure that is COMPUTABLE from a module is stated once in a gated block and pinned here, the same contract manifest.test.js holds over Testing.md's suite count: the effect catalog's row count and its inert subset off SPELL_MAKER_EFFECTS, and the guild service count and its unbuilt subset off SERVICE_DESTINATION, each failing from EITHER side with a message naming the culprit (the guild pin prints which services are still null). The block's own heading is pinned too, so the figures cannot stop being gated by being renamed. The fourth test is the interesting one: a re-swept row legitimately QUOTES the figure it corrects ("the title said SEVENTEEN until the re-sweep" is history, not a claim), so the rule is per LINE - a line mentioning a superseded figure must also carry a correction marker. A blunt phrase ban was tried first and flagged the corrections themselves, which is how the distinction got found. All four pins red-proofed: a drifted count, a superseded figure left standing, the block renamed, and the CODE moving while the doc did not |
| modalkeys.test.js | 5 | V4 - ONE MODAL OWNS THE KEYS, found by the first-hour playthrough probe in a SCREENSHOT: its first picture of the starting dungeon was the dungeon AUTOMAP, open over a 0%-explored Privateer's Hold on a game one minute old, and nobody had pressed the automap key - the character's name was MAC. The classic start runs the chargen wizard with the player already standing in the dungeon; the wizard lives in the OUTER host's overlay slot (world.js -> townTalk), world.js gates each of its own actions on `!townTalk.overlayActive`, and worldModes registered a SECOND keydown listener on the same target gated on nothing - so every key typed into the wizard also reached `routeKey(e, dungeonCtx)`, whose own overlay branch reads `ctx.uiOverlayActive` (the DUNGEON's slot, empty while the wizard is up) and waved them through to the bindings. Any letter in a name is a keybinding: M is the automap, R starts a rest, N the notebook, L the logbook, Z readies a weapon, F9 quicksaves a half-made character - and whatever opened was still up when the wizard closed. This is AUDIT 24's two-chargen-wizards defect at a second seam ('two listeners on the same target and neither stops propagation') and takes the same rule. Hosts have no node execution coverage, so the ORDERING half is a source rule over comment-stripped source - the yield must be present AND precede every branch worldModes handles itself - with two rules that stop it becoming vacuous: ui/input.js must stay host-agnostic (routeKey cannot see the outer overlay, which is WHY the host must yield, so this is not a duplicate of a check the router already makes) and world.js must still carry its own `!townTalk.overlayActive` gates, or the defect has merely moved hosts. The DANGER half is executable rather than asserted: the letters the probe actually typed are resolved through the real binding store and M really does answer AutoMap. RED-PROOFED BOTH WAYS - with the guard removed rules 1 and 2 fail and the live probe reports `overlay: AutomapWindow`; with it restored all five pass and the probe reports none. The live walk is tools/firstHourProbe.mjs |
| tdz.test.js | 2 | THE GAME DID NOT BOOT AND EVERY GATE WAS GREEN. `createWorldModes` read `say` at its line 222 and declared `const say = ...` at 263, so the first line of the game threw `ReferenceError: Cannot access 'say' before initialization` and the world host never reached a mode. Lint passed - a temporal dead zone is legal syntax and the identifier IS bound, so `no-undef` is blind to it - the vite build passed, and all 3005 tests passed, because nothing in the suite executes a host constructor. Only the first-hour playthrough probe found it, the only way it can be found: by starting the game. eslint.config.js's own header describes this shape one rung down ("unbound identifiers are invisible to node --check, vite build, and headless tests - no-undef catches the whole class"); this is that leg for the dead-zone class. NOT `no-use-before-define`: that flags 510 sites across src/ and almost all are legal, because a closure mentioning a const declared further down does not RUN until later and ESLint cannot tell a deferred reference from an immediate one. This gate can. It parses every file with rollup's own `parseAst` (already a dependency - no new one) and models LEXICAL scopes plus one bit per scope: `immediate` goes false the moment the walk crosses into a function body, so an outer binding read from in there is legal and only a reference evaluated in the same execution scope as the declaration it precedes is reported. The first draft got exactly this wrong in the other direction - it ignored block scoping, read every `for (const obj of ...)` in a file as one binding, and reported 427 phantom dead zones. Shorthand `{ say }` is counted ONCE (key and value are the same node, and it is the very read this exists for). RED-PROOFED ON THE REAL FILE: with the broken worldModes restored the gate names `src/scenes/worldModes.js:222 reads 'say', declared at :263` and nothing else; with the declaration moved above its use, src/ is clean. The second test is the gate's own conscience - it must report the reduced defect, must NOT report a deferred closure reference, and must not confuse a block-scoped shadow for the outer name |

The first-hour probe made it concrete: a character walked out of Privateer's Hold at 6/31 health, travelled to Burgley, found an innkeeper, rented a room for five gold - the gold left the purse, the rental record landed - and then pressing Rest in that room opened nothing. Two things were missing and only one was obvious. The obvious one: `ctx.toggleRest` (ui/input.js:106, the ONLY consumer of the Rest binding) had exactly one implementation in the tree, dungeonContext's, so KeyR was dead in three of the four hosts - RestWindow and RestSession have been finished since U7 and simply had nobody to open them. The one underneath: DaggerfallRestWindow.CanRest (:542-600) was unported ENTIRELY, and restSession.js' own header had said so since U7 - "Building trespass/rent rules pend towns" - four audits of a line that reads like a footnote and is the whole town half of resting. Pinned arm by arm: IsPlayerInTown's SEVEN town types (not the three called Town - HomeFarms, HomeWealthy, Tavern and ReligionTemple are towns here) and its mustBeOutside gate read the right way round, because backwards it inverts the law and every inn in Tamriel refuses a bed and charges vagrancy for asking; the two-step camping flow, whose return value IS `alreadyWarned` so the first press refuses and the confirmed one allows, with Vagrancy and the watch registered on BOTH paths (a player who backs out has still committed the crime, and with the warning setting off commits it on every press while never being allowed to camp); the rented-room arm including the stored bed index, its out-of-range fall to 0, and an EXPIRED room not being a room; the ship/owned-house early return and the permanent-scene gate that keeps an ordinary Ship-type shop from being a bed; and the guild-hall privilege with the TAVERN exclusion DFU explains itself - the data marks every tavern a Fighters Guild, so without it every common room is a free bed and nobody rents one. A DFU DEFECT IS RECORDED AND AVOIDED: :577-583 calls GetRemainingHours(room), which handles a null room, then reads `room.allocatedBedIndex` with no null check - an expired rental the sweep has not collected is a NullReferenceException there and simply fails the gate here. Two host rules close it: every host that can hold a player now has a rest arm and each goes through the LAW rather than re-deciding it, and ONE generic tick drives the window - RestWindow.tick forwards to tickRest so `overlay.tick?.(dt)` (which every host already calls) is enough, and the dungeon's explicit `if (isRestWindow) tickRest(dt)` had to GO or it would rest at double speed. That explicit branch was the same per-host-branch shape that made resting dungeon-only in the first place. The live proof is tools/firstHourProbe.mjs' bed stage + V5b: THE PAGE ERROR THIS LANE SHIPPED, AND THE ONE IT FOUND. TEXT.RSC answers `{ text, center }` ROWS (textRsc.linesById:216-247 - the record's own bytes carry justification) while RestWindow, ActionTextBox and ChoiceWindow all iterate the STRING, so handing `townTalk.lines(id)` to one ends in `TypeError: text is not iterable` from drawText. V5's first cut did exactly that and the rested night died on it; nothing in the suite draws, so only the first-hour probe's zero-page-errors gate caught it, at the very last stage. The rule written for that mistake then found one that was NOT V5's: the vampire message box - "Death is not eternal", TEXT.RSC 401 - takes whatever `textAt` answers and hands it to a ChoiceWindow, and THREE of its four providers answer rows (world.js, exterior.js, worldModes.js all pass `townTalk.lines`), while only dungeonContext passes `textRsc.plainText`, which answers strings. That popup threw on draw everywhere above ground and worked in a dungeon - the four-hosts divergence this project keeps meeting. Flattened at the ONE consumer so no provider has to be right. The source rule took THREE drafts and the red-proof caught the first two: draft one spelled `townTalk?.lines?.(` with optional dots, consumed the separator before `.lines`, matched nothing and passed on the very build it was written to reject; draft two flagged by PROXIMITY and then flagged the infection seam's own provider, which is safe precisely because the flatten moved downstream of it. It targets the sites that BUILD the line list |

Every test above passed because their fixtures set the key by hand: a synthetic fixture cannot catch a missing field that the fixture itself supplies. PurchaseHouse is the one transaction in banking that spends from BOTH pockets, and the mechanism is DeductGoldAmount's return value: it answers the SHORTFALL rather than nothing (court.js:199 ports it, letters of credit and all), so `accountGold -= deductGold(amount)` subtracts exactly the remainder and subtracts ZERO when the purse covered it - written any other way it double-charges or lets the account pay for what the purse already bought. Pinned three ways: purse-covers (account untouched), account-tops-up (purse to the last coin, account pays exactly the rest), and refused (NOTHING moves). STILL FLAGGED, and it is presentation not function: the 104x91 panel at (117,12) is a live 3D render of the selected building rotating one degree per 0.02s on a dedicated Unity camera and layer. The port draws the panel empty. Everything the window is FOR is here; what is missing is a picture |

The 30% weather bonus reads backwards and is right: `None` means ALWAYS, so the eleven princes with no condition are never penalised and the five with one - Sanguine, Peryite, Boethiah, Nocturnal want rain; Sheogorath wants thunder - are the only ones the sky can cost, by not being their weather; a storm is not rain and rain is not a storm, pinned both ways. The cost is the SUMMONER's reputation inverted (200000 - rep*1000), the most expensive service in the game by two orders of magnitude, and the gold is spent BEFORE the roll and never refunded - you paid for the summoning, not for the prince turning up. THE FRAME IS PART OF THE KEY, because DFU imports animated flats frame by frame: replacing frame 0 of a torch and nothing else leaves the rest classic, and the mutant that hands every frame frame 0's replacement dies on it. DECODE-AHEAD IS THE STRUCTURAL DIFFERENCE FROM MUSIC. uploadRecord is synchronous and runs off the draw path, while loading and decoding a PNG is neither - music could commit and arrive late because a song is continuous, but a texture is needed for the frame being drawn and a late arrival is a visible pop or a missing wall. So the work happens where there is already an await: getTexture() loads TEXTURE.### per archive, and that archive's replacements decode alongside it, BEFORE the archive is published, or a draw could beat them. Per archive and idempotent, both pinned, and one unreadable PNG costs that texture and leaves the pack working. The override sits BELOW the spectral arm on purpose: that path builds an albedo AND an emission mask from one remap, and replacing half of it would light a ghost by a texture it no longer wears. Plus: a new pick drops the DECODED cache too, or swapping packs shows the previous pack's art for anything the new one does not cover; the ingest and the picker are ONE implementation across both domains, because writing them twice is how the second one drifts; and the settings row's second action carries its own KEY (T), because that dialog has no button hit-testing to choose with and a button that looked clickable and did nothing would be the dead affordance this project keeps finding. 17 mutations, 17 dead + BOTH PACKS HAVE A URL DOOR: ?music shipped first and ?textures did not follow it, which left the texture pick reachable only from the settings row - the same asymmetry that makes a feature look absent. U51 RE-AIMED THE EXITS PIN AND ADDED THE RAIL PIN: the law there was never the COUNT of actions but that settings, mods and about are destinations INSIDE this screen rather than exits from it, so it states both halves now - the six that resolve (continue/new/load at boot, resume/save/exit in pause) and the three that must never be among them - and a second pin holds the two rails differing only where the QUESTION does: Continue and New Game ask 'which game', which is settled by the time the pause door mounts, Resume/Save/Exit ask 'what now', and the four shared rows (Load, Settings, Mods, About) must reach BOTH, because a pause rail without Settings would have left U49's own complaint true on the very skin built to close it. AND THE THREE FIXES LOOKING AT IT ON A PIXEL 5 FOUND. THE PHONE RAIL WRAPS rather than scrolls: it is a flex row with overflow-x: auto and its scrollbar hidden, so four of seven destinations sat off-screen with no affordance at all - Settings and Exit among them - which is the AUDIT 24 shape exactly, a control that is drawn, exists, and cannot be reached on the device that needs it most. SIX did not fit either, so the front door had carried the bug since U49 and nobody had seen it, because the entry it hid was About rather than Exit; the wizard's rail is exempted and pinned as exempt, because that one is a WALK through ten stages in order and a walk that wraps stops reading as a line. A READING COLUMN: .body had no width, so a card of three lines stretched the full width of a desktop pane and every screen but Settings read as mostly empty - capped at 720px, with .body.flush pinned UNcapped because the settings pane owns its own three columns. ONE SLOT DRAWN ONE WAY: four panes now render the same single quicksave (Continue, Load, Save, Exit), so the character line and the health/gold numbers are written ONCE and the pin counts the literals to prove no second copy grew back. The live killer for the rail is tools/enhancedPauseProbe.mjs, which measures every rail button against the viewport and names the ones outside it - reachable and VISIBLE are different claims, and the 44px target check passes either way. 5 further mutations, 5 dead |

The province names are the map's own spelling, SUMURSET included, so the mutation that 'corrects' it fails. THE RAIL CANNOT DRIFT FROM THE FLOW: every state in ui/chargen.js's own STATES belongs to exactly one rail stage and the rail names no state the wizard does not have, checked against the source rather than a copy. And THE KEYBOARD's three quiet laws, whose live proof is tools/enhancedChargenProbe.mjs walking the whole wizard to `done` without a pointer event: keys route through overlayAction, the SHARED table (a second key map is how two skins come to disagree about what Escape does), the window-level listener is REMOVED on unmount (it outlives the DOM it was mounted for), and preventDefault comes AFTER the table has claimed the key so Tab keeps moving focus - plus the INPUT guard, because the name boxes feed the flow themselves and a stolen key is a doubled letter. Live proof of the mount is tools/enhancedIntegrationProbe.mjs, which boots the real game. And A MODAL OVERLAY OWNS ITS INPUT, from Mac playing the deployed build - the wizard came up over a live dungeon, the map ignored every click and the mouse still swung the view, which are the same fact twice: a LOCKED pointer never reaches the DOM (every mouse event goes to the locked element as a delta, so a fixed div is invisible to it at any z-index) and the hosts only gate RE-taking the lock, nothing released one already held. The view drops the lock and keeps dropping it, its keydown is on CAPTURE and stops there so the host never sees a key the wizard used, and a key the wizard did NOT use still reaches the page. And A REPAINT MUST NOT MOVE THE PAGE UNDER THE PLAYER, from Mac playing on a phone - every tap on a skills stepper threw the list back to the top, which is the hover bug one layer up: these screens rebuild their whole DOM on every state change, and there the destroyed thing was the node under the pointer while here it is the scroll offset. Both enhanced screens repaint through ui/domRepaint.js, and the sweep says so because a repaint written straight takes the bug back and looks like a simplification. THE HELPER'S OWN BEHAVIOUR IS NOT PINNABLE HERE - node has no DOM - and the mutation that makes it record nothing SURVIVES the suite by design; its killer is tools/enhancedScrollProbe.mjs, which taps with a real touchscreen and exits 1 on the mutant (300 -> 0) and 0 on the fix (300 -> 300). That probe took three tries to write and all three failures were the probe: twice it tapped a control scrolled out of view, then it tapped a DECREMENT on a value at its rolled floor, which the law correctly refuses - so nothing moved, the scroll held, and it reported the bug fixed while it was still there. It checks the tap moved something before believing anything now. 17 mutations, 17 dead in the suite + 1 recorded survivor whose killer is the probe |

BEHAVIOUR, because the fork itself runs in node: `?skin=` is a URL and uiSkin reads one from an injected string, so both branches are taken here for real - classic gets PauseOptionsWindow, enhanced gets the DOM overlay with its id, its fixed opaque cssText and `done === false` on arrival, and dispose closes it and takes the div with it. THE FIRST DRAFT OF THAT PIN WAS VACUOUS, and the mutation sweep is what found it: the fork's second clause is `typeof document !== 'undefined'` and node has none, so BOTH skins fell to the classic branch and the pin passed for the wrong reason - dropping `isEnhanced()` from the condition SURVIVED. It runs under a four-property fake document now (createElement, body.append, and a node with an id, a style and a remove), which is exactly what the enhanced branch touches before it goes async, and the mutation dies twice over. THE ART GATE, which moved with the door: classic with no OPTN00I0 has nothing to draw and pauseDoorReady answers false, the enhanced screen reads no game data at all and it answers true - gating it on classic art would strand a player whose art load failed with no pause menu, no settings and no way out of a game that would have rendered perfectly. THE RACE THE GUARD IS FOR, driven for real: dispose BEFORE the lazy import lands, then settle the import and prove nothing mounted - without the guard the mount runs into a div already off the document and the menu it builds has no owner, so the capture keydown that would have been removed on unmount eats Escape for the rest of the session and the game can never be paused again. SOURCE SWEEPS, and they say so: every host gates on pauseDoorReady, imports ui/pauseDoor.js and does NOT import the classic window past the fork; the door mounts ui/enhancedMenu.js in PAUSE MODE rather than a second design, and holds no layout of its own; the import is DYNAMIC so a player who chose classic never loads a byte of the enhanced screen, and a failed load takes its empty div with it rather than leaving the host an overlay that never reports done - a frozen game; `done` goes true only AFTER unmount and remove; every exit closes before it acts; the five host arms are no-ops BY DESIGN and each says why, because a silently empty input() is indistinguishable from a broken one; Escape routes through overlayAction (the shared table, not a second key map) with its back stack innermost-first - confirm, then the phone's help sheet, then the screen - and stopPropagation, because the host walks the player on the keys underneath it; every listener has an owner; the pointer lock is dropped and kept dropped; and the panes answer the HOST rather than the rail, with the mount proven to KEEP the hooks it was handed (dropping them is silent - all four hosts lose save, load and exit at once, and each politely says this part of the game has no door). The pin holds the line's address against inventoryItemImage's for every item, and a second one holds the WEARER travelling - a model that takes the argument and a view that forgets to pass it is the same bug one layer up, so it sweeps both call sites too.  Two more came out of reading the U54 diff adversarially before pushing it, and both were real: the template-name FALLBACK went away with the template read it replaced, so an item minted with no name of its own (a loot roll, a quest) said "Unknown" instead of "Longsword"; and the icon carried a width ATTRIBUTE, which forces every sprite to 30 across when a dagger is tall and narrow and a cuirass is wide. The CSS caps both axes instead, and the pin reads the rule. |

Two tiers per module:
1. **Synthetic fixtures** - in-memory data built inside the test. Always run;
   CI stays green with no game data (88 pass, 49 skip).
2. **Real-data validation** - gated on `ARENA2_PATH`; skip cleanly when
   absent. Pin observed counts, names, ids, checksums, and structural
   closure invariants.

Without game data the suite runs 805 pass / 120 skip (AUDIT 18 corrected
the long-stale "88 pass, 49 skip", which described a 137-test suite that
has not existed for many milestones). Those 75 skipped pins DO NOT RUN
IN THE DEPLOY GATE - CI has no ARENA2 - so a real-data pin is a local
gate only. (Measured on main at the AUDIT 18 merge; recount by hand
when the split moves.) `manifest.test.js` guards the totals above but not this line;
recount it by hand when the split moves.

Sourcing data in a fresh session: `sh tools/fetch-data.sh`, then
`ARENA2_PATH=/home/claude/dfdata/arena2 npm test`.

Visual proof: `npm run shot [out.png]` boots vite in-process and drives the
provisioned Chromium (playwright@1.56.0 <-> /opt/pw-browsers chromium-1194;
bump both together). CONTRACT: the scene query rides the SHOT_QUERY env
var, NOT argv (`SHOT_QUERY='shot&world&tod=22:00' npm run shot out.png`) -
argv[2] is only the output path. SHOT_TIMEOUT overrides the 30s ready
wait (streaming world wants 90000); SHOT_EVAL runs a JS snippet in-page
after ready (promise-returning expressions are awaited - top-level
`await` fails). Shot-mode probe hooks in the dungeon and world scenes:
window.__move(dx, dy, dz), window.__pose(x, y, z, yaw, pitch),
window.__player {pos, warp}, and in the dungeon __activate/__activateKey/
__ray/__actions. SwiftShader renders heavy scenes at ~7 fps headless -
time-based proofs must poll state, never trust wall-clock. `?shot` in main.js fixes the vantage and raises
`window.__shotReady`. Manual proof, not a suite gate.

Playing the game: `ARENA2_PATH=... npm run firsthour` drives ONE character
through one continuous session on the real hosts - chargen, the starting
dungeon, a fight, the loot, the way out, the road, a town, a shop, a
purchase, a bed - asserting the player is alive and the state coherent at
each step, with a screenshot per stage (`SHOTS=<dir>`, default
`firsthour-shots/`). A failing stage does NOT stop the walk; its dependents
are marked skipped and the run continues, because the output is a MAP of
what a first hour hits rather than the first pothole. `ONLY_CHARGEN=1` stops
after the wizard - a four-minute run instead of twenty-five, which is how
the modal-ownership fix was red-proofed. It is a TOOL, not a suite gate: it
needs game data and about twenty-five minutes of SwiftShader. Run it when a
host, a window or the boot path changes - `tools/nativeTradeProbe.mjs` sat
red for months because nothing did.

Pre-push gate: `npm run check` (test + build).

Drift guard: `test/manifest.test.js` pins the total line and every row of
the table above against the real suite. Recalculate this doc in the same
commit as any test change.
| verifydeploy.test.js | 11 | V-DEPLOY - THE VERIFIER'S OWN CORRECTNESS, and it had been giving the wrong answer in the most ordinary case there is. tools/verify-deploy.mjs decides whether a push actually reached the players, and it decided it by comparing the ENTRY CHUNK'S HASH - which only holds while YOUR build tag is the head CI happened to build. Other sessions push to main constantly, so the deploy that lands is routinely a DESCENDANT of your commit: your work is on the site, the hash belongs to somebody else, and the tool polls for eight minutes and reports TIMEOUT. FOUR CYCLES were spent re-running a red verifier against perfectly good deploys, each time confirming ancestry by hand with `git merge-base --is-ancestor` and then rebuilding at the true head. Doing by hand what a tool reports wrongly is the signal to fix the tool. THE ARTIFACT NOW SAYS WHAT COMMIT IT IS: vite.config.js stamps `<meta name="build-tag">` into every built page from the sha scripts/buildTag.mjs already writes into src/buildTag.js, so the verifier can read one attribute out of the live index instead of parsing a minified rollup chunk whose variable name is gone. Both halves are pinned against EACH OTHER rather than against a hand-written string - the plugin is pulled out of the real config, called, and its output fed to the real reader - so a rename on either side fails here. THE VERDICT LADDER IS FOUR ARMS AND THE ORDER IS THE WHOLE POINT. Exact hash first, so an UNSTAMPED pair on both sides still verifies the way it always did (demoting it makes six pins fail). Then SAME COMMIT, DIFFERENT CHUNK, which is a DIRTY LOCAL BUILD and fails outright - this arm has to stand ahead of the ancestry question because git answers that a commit is an ancestor of ITSELF, so without it a dist/ built from a dirty tree reports VERIFIED against a deploy that does not contain the uncommitted changes, which is precisely the lie the tool exists to prevent, and the pin asserts the oracle is never even CONSULTED. Then ancestry, asked in ONE direction (reversed, a stale site passes) and accepted only on `=== true`, because git exits 128 on a commit this clone has never fetched and CANNOT TELL IS NOT A YES - the oracle answers null there, and the runner re-asks once after a fetch. Last, an unstamped LIVE page never reaches the ancestry question at all: there is no commit to compare, so the only honest answer is the old behaviour. And the file is a LIBRARY UNLESS IT IS THE PROGRAM, split the way tools/musicNames.mjs was split after its top-level process.exit killed the runner mid-file and reported a green suite - the guard is pinned as the statement that gates main(), not as a string that appears somewhere in the file, because that mutant survives. 14 mutations, 14 dead |
